y=g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon t_{j}-\varepsilon T),\quad\text{and}\quad y^{\prime}=g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon s_{2}-\varepsilon T)

so that
\mathchoice{d\left(y,p_{j}\right)}{d(y,p_{j})}{d(y,p_{j})}{d(y,p_{j})}\leqslant\alpha
and
\mathchoice{d\left(y^{\prime},q_{2}\right)}{d(y^{\prime},q_{2})}{d(y^{\prime},q_{2})}{d(y^{\prime},q_{2})}\leqslant\alpha
.
Recall that
q_{2}=g\gamma(\varepsilon s_{2})
is a projection of
c_{2}
on
g\gamma
. Combining
Lemma 3.9
with (
10
) we get

\mathchoice{\left\langle y,c_{2}\right\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}{\langle y,c_{2}\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}{\langle y,c_{2}\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}{\langle y,c_{2}\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left\langle p_{j},c_{2}\right\rangle_{q_{2}}}{\langle p_{j},c_{2}\rangle_{q_{2}}}{\langle p_{j},c_{2}\rangle_{q_{2}}}{\langle p_{j},c_{2}\rangle_{q_{2}}}+2\alpha\leqslant 7\alpha.

Similarly, the triangle inequality yields

\mathchoice{\left\langle u_{j}o,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{y}}{\langle u_{j}o,y^{\prime}\rangle_{y}}{\langle u_{j}o,y^{\prime}\rangle_{y}}{\langle u_{j}o,y^{\prime}\rangle_{y}}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left\langle p_{j},q_{2}\right\rangle_{p_{j}}}{\langle p_{j},q_{2}\rangle_{p_{j}}}{\langle p_{j},q_{2}\rangle_{p_{j}}}{\langle p_{j},q_{2}\rangle_{p_{j}}}+3\alpha\leqslant 3\alpha.

Thus
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
are
7\alpha
-separated by
(y,y^{\prime})
.
Note that

s_{2}-T\geqslant M-(L+r+197\alpha)\quad\text{and}\quad T-t_{j}\geqslant L+150\alpha

Hence
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
are
(L+120\alpha,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g^{\prime}
(
Proposition 3.18
).
Combining (
9
) and (
10
) we see that

\mathchoice{d\left(\gamma_{j}(t+T-T_{j}),g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon t)\right)}{d(\gamma_{j}(t+T-T_{j}),g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon t))}{d(\gamma_{j}(t+T-T_{j}),g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon t))}{d(\gamma_{j}(t+T-T_{j}),g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon t))}\leqslant 2\alpha,\quad\forall t\in\mathbf{R}.

Note that
T-T_{j}\geqslant L
.
It follows directly that
g^{\prime}o
belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{x_{j}}(x^{\prime}_{j},2\alpha)
.
Consequently
(u_{0},\dots,u_{j},g^{\prime})
is an
L
-admissible sequence.

Case 2.

Suppose now that
u_{j}^{-1}g
does not belong to
E(h)
.

According to
Lemma 5.7
,
o
and
c_{1}
are
(L,36\alpha,\varepsilon_{j})
-separated at
u_{j}
.
It follows from
Lemma 5.2
that
t_{j}\leqslant-M+(r+22\alpha)
.
Let
m=g\gamma(\varepsilon\theta)
be a projection of
x^{\prime}_{j}
on
g\gamma
.
Since
u_{j}\gamma
has a small projection on
g\gamma
– see
(H3)
– we get

\theta\leqslant t_{j}+\alpha\leqslant-M+(r+23\alpha)

By definition of admissible sequences, there is a point
z^{\prime}\in B(u_{j+1}o,10\alpha)
such that
\mathchoice{\left\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}\leqslant 2\alpha
.
The projection on a contracting set being large scale Lipschitz (
Remark 2.1
), the projection
m^{\prime}=g\gamma(\varepsilon\theta^{\prime})
of
z^{\prime}
on
g\gamma
satisfies

\theta^{\prime}\geqslant t_{j+1}-14\alpha\geqslant 26\alpha.

In particular,
x^{\prime}_{j}
and
z^{\prime}
are
2\alpha
-separated by
(m,m^{\prime})
.
It follows from
Proposition 3.18

(vi)
that
go
is
5\alpha
-close to a point
z
on a geodesic
\nu
joining
x^{\prime}_{j}
to
z^{\prime}
.
Hence the triangle inequality yields

\mathchoice{\left\langle x_{j},z\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}{\langle x_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}_{j}}}+\mathchoice{\left\langle x^{\prime}_{j},z^{\prime}\right\rangle_{z}}{\langle x^{\prime}_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{z}}{\langle x^{\prime}_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{z}}{\langle x^{\prime}_{j},z^{\prime}\rangle_{z}}\leqslant 2\alpha.

In other words
z
belongs to
B(go,10\alpha)\cap\mathcal{O}_{x_{j}}(x^{\prime}_{j},2\alpha)
.
Consequently the sequence
(u_{0},\dots,u_{j},g)
is
L
-admissible.
Let us prove now that
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
are separated at
g
.
Recall that
p_{j}=g\gamma(\varepsilon t_{j})
and
q_{2}=g\gamma(\varepsilon s_{2})
are respective projections of
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
on
g\gamma
.
It follows
Lemma 3.9
that
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
are
5\alpha
-separated by
(p_{j},q_{2})
.
We already noticed that
t_{j}\leqslant-M+(r+22\alpha)
while
s_{2}\geqslant M-(r+7\alpha)
.
Consequently
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
are
(L+120\alpha,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
(
Proposition 3.18
).

Conclusion.

In both cases we have produced an element
g^{\prime}\in g\left\langle h\right\rangle
such that
(u_{0},\dots,u_{j},g^{\prime})
is an
L
-admissible sequence while
u_{j}o
and
c_{2}
are
(L+120\alpha,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g^{\prime}
.
According to
Proposition 5.8
, we can extend
(u_{0},\dots,u_{j},g^{\prime})
to an infinite
L
-admissible sequence from
1
to
[c_{2}]
.
Thus
u
belongs to
C_{L}(c_{2})
.
∎

5.4
Visual metric

Let
L>200\alpha
.
Given
c_{1},c_{2}\in\Lambda
, we define an auxiliary quantity
r_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})
.
It is the largest number
r\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
with the following properties:

(i)

there exists
g\in C_{L}(c_{1})\cap C_{L}(c_{2})
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}=r
, and

(ii)

C_{L}(c_{1})\cap B_{G}(r)=C_{L}(c_{2})\cap B_{G}(r)
.

If
C_{L}(c_{1})=C_{L}(c_{2})
we adopt the convention that
r_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})=\infty
.
Otherwise, the existence of
r_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})
follows from the fact that the action of
G
on
X
is proper.
Roughly speaking
r_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})
is the radius of the largest ball on which
C_{L}(c_{1})
and
C_{L}(c_{2})
coincide.

Definition 5.13
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
The
L
-visual pseudo-metric
between
c_{1},c_{2}\in\Lambda
(seen from
o
) is

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}=e^{-r_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}.

Remark
.

Observe that
d_{L}
satisfies an ultra-metric inequality, namely

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{3}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{3})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{3})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{3})}\leqslant\max\left\{\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})},\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{2},c_{3}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{2},c_{3})}{d_{L}(c_{2},c_{3})}{d_{L}(c_{2},c_{3})}\right\},\quad\forall c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}\in\Lambda.

∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
The visual pseudo-metric
d_{L}
on
\Lambda
induces an ultra-metric distance on
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
.

Proof.

By construction the visual pseudo-distance between two cocycles in
\Lambda
only depends on their respective equivalence classes.
Hence
d_{L}
induces a pseudo-distance on
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
.
The only point left to check is that
d_{L}
is positive.
Let
c_{1},c_{2}\in\Lambda
such that
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}=0
.
By definition this means that
C_{L}(c_{1})=C_{L}(c_{2})
.
Let
R\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
and
g\in C_{L}(c_{1})
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}>R+17\alpha
.
Lemmas
5.4
and
5.7
tell us that
g
belongs to
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}(L-18\alpha)
while both
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
lie in
\mathcal{O}_{o}(go,4\alpha)
.
It follows from
[
9
, Lemma 5.7]
that
\mathchoice{\left\|c_{1}-c_{2}\right\|_{K}}{\|c_{1}-c_{2}\|_{K}}{\|c_{1}-c_{2}\|_{K}}{\|c_{1}-c_{2}\|_{K}}\leqslant 20\alpha
, where
K
is the closed ball of radius
R
centered at
o
.
This fact holds for every
R\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, thus
c_{1}\sim c_{2}
.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
r,L,M\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, with
L>200\alpha
and
M>2L+r+500\alpha
.
Let
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
.
Let
c_{1},c_{2}\in\Lambda
.
Let
g\in G
.
If
o
and
\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
are
(M,r,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
, then

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}.

Proof.

Let
R=\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}
.
Note that
(1,g)
is an
L
-admissible sequence (the condition to check is void).
Using
Proposition 5.8
, we can extend
(1,g)
to an infinity
L
-admissible sequence from
1
to
[c_{1}]
( respectively
[c_{2}]
).
Thus
g
belongs to both
C_{L}(c_{1})
and
C_{L}(c_{2})
.

Applying
Proposition 5.11
, we see that
C_{L}(c_{1})
and
C_{L}(c_{2})
coincide on
B_{G}(R)
.
Moreover this ball contains
g
.
By definition of the
L
-visual pseudo-metric we get

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}.\qed

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
Let
c\in\Lambda
and
c^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{+}
, which are not equivalent.
For every
M\in\mathbf{R}
and
\eta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
, there is
g\in G
such that
c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant M
and

\max\left\{\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc,c_{\gamma}\right)}{d_{L}(gc,c_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc,c_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc,c_{\gamma})},\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma}\right)}{d_{L}(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}\right\}<\eta.

Proof.

Fix
T>2L+504\alpha
.
By
(H5)
, there is
n\in\mathbf{Z}
such that

\mathchoice{d\left(h^{n}\gamma(t),\gamma(t+T)\right)}{d(h^{n}\gamma(t),\gamma(t+T))}{d(h^{n}\gamma(t),\gamma(t+T))}{d(h^{n}\gamma(t),\gamma(t+T))}\leqslant\alpha.

(11)

In particular,
\mathchoice{d\left(o,h^{n}o\right)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}\geqslant T-\alpha
.
According to
Proposition 4.13
, there is
g\in G
such that
c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant M
while
c
and
c^{\prime}
are
(2T,2\alpha,+1)
-separated at
g^{-1}
.
In particular,
gc^{\prime}
and
c^{\prime}_{\gamma}
both belong to
\mathcal{O}_{o}(x^{\prime},2\alpha)
where
x^{\prime}=\gamma(2T)
.
Combined with (
11
) we get that
o
and
\{gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma}\}
are
(T,4\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
h^{n}
.
Hence by
Proposition 5.15
,

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma}\right)}{d_{L}(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,h^{n}o\right)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}}\leqslant e^{\alpha}e^{-T}.

The same proof gives an upper estimate of
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc,c_{\gamma}\right)}{d_{L}(gc,c_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc,c_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(gc,c_{\gamma})}
.
We conclude by noticing that
T
can be chosen arbitrary large.
∎

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
Let
c_{1},c_{2},c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
c_{i}
is not equivalent to
c^{\prime}
.
For every
\eta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
, there is
M\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that for every
g\in G
, if
c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant M
, then
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc_{1},gc_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}<\eta
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
\eta\in(0,1)
.
Fix
T>2L+524\alpha
.
Recall that
\Lambda=\Lambda_{-}\cup\Lambda_{+}
.
Let
\varepsilon=-1
( respectively
\varepsilon=+1
) if
c^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{-}
( respectively
c^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{+})
.
According to
Proposition 4.13
, there is
u\in G
such that
\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
and
c^{\prime}
are
(T,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
u
.
Fix

M=-\ln\eta+c^{\prime}(o,uo)+T+10\alpha.

Let
g\in G
such that
c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant M
, that is
c^{\prime}(uo,g^{-1}o)\geqslant-\ln\eta+T+10\alpha
.
Denote by
p=u\gamma(\varepsilon s)
and
q=u\gamma(\varepsilon t)
be projection of
g^{-1}o
and
c^{\prime}
on
u\gamma
restricted to
\mathchoice{\left[-T\,,T\right]}{[-T\,,T]}{[-T\,,T]}{[-T\,,T]}
.
We know by
Proposition 3.18
that
t\geqslant T-9\alpha
.
We claim that
s\geqslant T-13\alpha
.
Indeed, if this is not the case then
\mathchoice{d\left(p,q\right)}{d(p,q)}{d(p,q)}{d(p,q)}>4\alpha
.
Using
Lemma 3.9
we observe

\displaystyle c^{\prime}(uo,g^{-1}o)\leqslant c^{\prime}(uo,q)-c^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,q)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathchoice{d\left(uo,q\right)}{d(uo,q)}{d(uo,q)}{d(uo,q)}-\mathchoice{d\left(g^{-1}o,q\right)}{d(g^{-1}o,q)}{d(g^{-1}o,q)}{d(g^{-1}o,q)}+10\alpha

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathchoice{d\left(uo,q\right)}{d(uo,q)}{d(uo,q)}{d(uo,q)}+10\alpha

\displaystyle\leqslant T+10\alpha,

which contradicts our assumption.
Note that
\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
and
g^{-1}o
are
2\alpha
-separated by
(y,p)
, where
y=u\gamma(-\varepsilon T)
.
Hence
\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
and
g^{-1}o
are
(T-22\alpha,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
u
(
Proposition 3.18
).
Or equivalently
\{gc_{1},gc_{2}\}
and
o
are
(T-22\alpha,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
gu
.
According to
Proposition 5.15
we get

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc_{1},gc_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,guo\right)}{d(o,guo)}{d(o,guo)}{d(o,guo)}}

However

\mathchoice{d\left(o,guo\right)}{d(o,guo)}{d(o,guo)}{d(o,guo)}\geqslant\mathchoice{d\left(g^{-1}o,uo\right)}{d(g^{-1}o,uo)}{d(g^{-1}o,uo)}{d(g^{-1}o,uo)}\geqslant c^{\prime}(uo,g^{-1}o)>-\ln\eta,

whence the result.
∎

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
Let
b,b^{\prime}\in\Lambda
which are not equivalent.
There are
r_{1},r^{\prime}_{1},\beta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
with the following property.
For every
c,c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(b,c\right)}{d_{L}(b,c)}{d_{L}(b,c)}{d_{L}(b,c)}<r_{1}
and
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\right)}{d_{L}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}{d_{L}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}{d_{L}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}<r^{\prime}_{1}
, the point
o
is
\beta
-close to any complete gradient line from
c
to
c^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}\leqslant\beta
.

Proof.

Fix
M>2L+504\alpha
.
According to
Proposition 4.13
, there are
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}
and
g^{\prime}\in G
such that
\{b,o\}
and
b^{\prime}
are
(M,2\alpha,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-separated at
g^{\prime}
.
For simplicity, we write
\gamma_{0}
for the path
\gamma
restricted to
\mathchoice{\left[-M\,,M\right]}{[-M\,,M]}{[-M\,,M]}{[-M\,,M]}
.
Similarly there are
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
and
g\in G
such that
b
and
\{o,b^{\prime}\}\cup g^{\prime}\gamma_{0}
are
(M,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
We show as in the proof of
Proposition 5.15
that
g
and
g^{\prime}
respectively belong to
C_{L}(c)
and
C_{L}(c^{\prime})
.
Set

x=g\gamma(-\varepsilon L),\quad y=g\gamma(\varepsilon L),\quad x^{\prime}=g^{\prime}\gamma(-\varepsilon^{\prime}L),\quad\text{and}\quad y^{\prime}=g^{\prime}\gamma(\varepsilon^{\prime}L).

In addition, let by
m=g\gamma(0)
ans
m^{\prime}=g^{\prime}\gamma(0)
.
Fix also
r_{1},r^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
such that

r_{1}<e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}\quad\text{and}\quad r^{\prime}_{1}<e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,g^{\prime}o\right)}{d(o,g^{\prime}o)}{d(o,g^{\prime}o)}{d(o,g^{\prime}o)}}.

Consider now
c,c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(b,c\right)}{d_{L}(b,c)}{d_{L}(b,c)}{d_{L}(b,c)}<r_{1}
and
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\right)}{d_{L}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}{d_{L}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}{d_{L}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}<r^{\prime}_{1}
.
We claim that
c
and
c^{\prime}
are
(L,36\alpha,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-separated at
g^{\prime}
.
It follows from the definition of the visual metric that
g
belongs to
C_{L}(b)\cap C_{L}(c)
.
Hence
o
and
\{b,c\}
are
(L,36\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
(
Lemma 5.7
).
Thus
\{b,c\}
and
\{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\}
are
(L,36\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
According to
Proposition 3.18
,
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}\leqslant 4\alpha
and
\mathchoice{\left\langle b,x^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}\leqslant 4\alpha
.
Observe also that

\mathchoice{\left\langle c,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}+\mathchoice{\left\langle c,x^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}+\mathchoice{\left\langle b,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}=\mathchoice{\left\langle b,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}+\mathchoice{\left\langle b,x^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle b,x^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}+\mathchoice{\left\langle c,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{m}},

(it suffices to unwrap the Gromov product).
By construction
b
and
b^{\prime}
are
(M,2\alpha,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-separated at
g^{\prime}
so that
\mathchoice{\left\langle b,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle b,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}\leqslant 2\alpha
.
It follows then from our previous discussion that
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,y^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle c,y^{\prime}\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}\leqslant 10\alpha
.
Similarly, one shows that
g^{\prime}
belongs to
C_{L}(c^{\prime})
and therefore
\mathchoice{\left\langle x^{\prime},c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}{\langle x^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}{\langle x^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}{\langle x^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle_{y^{\prime}}}\leqslant 36\alpha
(
Lemma 5.7
).
This completes the proof of our claim.
It now follows from
Proposition 3.18
that
m
lies in the
5\alpha
-neighborhood of any complete gradient line
\nu
from
c
to
c^{\prime}
.
In addition
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}\leqslant 4\alpha
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle d(o,\nu)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathchoice{d\left(o,m^{\prime}\right)}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}+d(m^{\prime},\nu)\leqslant\mathchoice{d\left(o,m^{\prime}\right)}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}+5\alpha,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{m^{\prime}}}+\mathchoice{d\left(o,m^{\prime}\right)}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}\leqslant\mathchoice{d\left(o,m^{\prime}\right)}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}{d(o,m^{\prime})}+4\alpha.

Note that the upper bounds do not depend on
c
nor on
c^{\prime}
, whence the result.
∎

The various
L
-visual metric are related as follows.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
\ell,L\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
with
\ell>200\alpha
and
L>2\ell+600\alpha
.
For every
c_{1},c_{2}\in\Lambda
we have
\mathchoice{d_{\ell}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{\ell}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\ell}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\ell}(c_{1},c_{2})}\leqslant\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}
.

Proof.

By definition of the visual metric, there is
g\in C_{L}(c_{1})\cap C_{L}(c_{2})
such that

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}=e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}.

In particular, there is
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that
o
and
\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
are
(L,36\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
(
Lemma 5.7
).
It follows then from
Proposition 5.15
that

\mathchoice{d_{\ell}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{\ell}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\ell}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\ell}(c_{1},c_{2})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}\leqslant\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{L}(c_{1},c_{2})}.\qed

In general,
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{L})
may not be a complete metric space, even if
L
is sufficiently large.
To bypass this difficulty, we combine all the
L
-visual metric together as follows.

Definition 5.20
.

The
visual pseudo-metric
between
c_{1},c_{2}\in\Lambda
(seen from
o
) is

\mathchoice{d_{\infty}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{\infty}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\infty}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\infty}(c_{1},c_{2})}=\sum_{k>200}2^{-k}\mathchoice{d_{k\alpha}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}

Remark 5.5
.

It follows from
Lemma 5.19
, that there is
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
such that for every integer
k>200
we have

2^{-k}\mathchoice{d_{k\alpha}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}\leqslant\mathchoice{d_{\infty}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{\infty}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\infty}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{\infty}(c_{1},c_{2})}\leqslant\mathchoice{d_{k\alpha}\!\left(c_{1},c_{2}\right)}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}{d_{k\alpha}(c_{1},c_{2})}+\lambda 2^{-k/2}.

Moreover, according to
Lemma 5.14
,
d_{\infty}
induces a metric on
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
.
Note that
d_{\infty}
may not be an ultra-metric though.
∎

Lemma 5.21
.

Let
(c_{n})
be a sequence of cocycles in
\Lambda
.
Assume that
(c_{n})
is a Cauchy sequence for the visual pseudo-metric
d_{\infty}
.
Any accumulation point
c^{*}
of
(c_{n})
for the topology of
\partial X
belongs to
\Lambda
.
Moreover

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathchoice{d_{\infty}\!\left(c_{n},c^{*}\right)}{d_{\infty}(c_{n},c^{*})}{d_{\infty}(c_{n},c^{*})}{d_{\infty}(c_{n},c^{*})}=0.

Proof.

Let
c^{*}
be an accumulation point of
(c_{n})
in
\partial X
.
Let
L>200\alpha
and
M>2L+600\alpha
.
Let
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
.
In view of
Lemma 5.19
,
(c_{n})
is also a Cauchy sequence for
d_{M}
.
Hence there is
n\in\mathbf{N}
, such that

\mathchoice{d_{M}\!\left(c_{n},c_{m}\right)}{d_{M}(c_{n},c_{m})}{d_{M}(c_{n},c_{m})}{d_{M}(c_{n},c_{m})}<e^{-T},\quad\forall m\geqslant n.

By definition of the visual metric, there is an element
g\in G
with
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}>T
such that for every
m\geqslant n
we have
g\in C_{M}(c_{n})\cap C_{M}(c_{m})
.
In particular,
g
belongs to
U(M-18\alpha)\subset U(L)
and
\mathchoice{\left\langle o,c_{m}\right\rangle_{go}}{\langle o,c_{m}\rangle_{go}}{\langle o,c_{m}\rangle_{go}}{\langle o,c_{m}\rangle_{go}}\leqslant 4\alpha
, for every
m\geqslant N
(Lemmas
5.4
and
5.7
).
Passing to the limit we get
c^{*}\in\mathcal{O}_{o}(go,4\alpha)
.
Note that the argument works for every
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
and every
L>120\alpha
.
Hence
c^{*}
belongs to
\Lambda_{U}(G,o,4\alpha)
hence to
\Lambda
.

We now prove the metric convergence.
We keep the same notations as above.
According to
Lemma 5.7
, there is
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that for all integers
m,m^{\prime}\geqslant n
, the point
o
and
\{c_{m},c_{m^{\prime}}\}
are
(M,36\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
Recall that separation is a closed property, hence
o
and
\{c_{m},c^{*}\}
are
(M,36\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
It follows now from
Proposition 5.15
that

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{m},c^{*}\right)}{d_{L}(c_{m},c^{*})}{d_{L}(c_{m},c^{*})}{d_{L}(c_{m},c^{*})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}<e^{-T},\quad\forall m\geqslant n.

This argument holds for every
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
.
Hence
(c_{n})
converges to
c^{*}
for the metric
d_{L}
.
This fact holds for every
L>200\alpha
.
We conclude from the dominated convergence theorem that
\mathchoice{d_{\infty}\!\left(c_{n},c^{*}\right)}{d_{\infty}(c_{n},c^{*})}{d_{\infty}(c_{n},c^{*})}{d_{\infty}(c_{n},c^{*})}
converges to zero.
∎

Proposition 5.22
.

The quotient
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{\infty})
is a complete separable metric space, hence a Polish space.

Proof.

Recall that
\partial X
is compact.
The completeness of
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{\infty})
is thus a direct consequence of
Lemma 5.21
.
Let us prove now that this space is separable.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, let
M_{k}=(2k+600)\alpha
.
For every integer
k>200
,
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
, and
g\in G
, we choose, if possible, a cocycle
c_{k,\varepsilon,g}\in\Lambda
such that
o
and
c_{k,\varepsilon,g}
are
(M_{k},2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
Denote by
Z
the set of all cocycles obtained in this way.
Since
G
is countable, so is
Z
.
We claim that the set
Z
is dense in
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{\infty})
.
Denote by
\lambda
the constant defined in
Remark 5.5
.
Consider
c\in\Lambda
and
\eta\in(0,1)
.
Choose an integer
k>200
such that
\lambda 2^{-k/2}\leqslant\eta/2
and set
T=-\ln(\eta/2)
.
Recall that
c\in\Lambda
.
According to
Proposition 4.13
, there is
g\in G
with
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}\geqslant T
and
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that
o
and
c
are
(M_{k},2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
Hence
o
and
\{c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g}\}
are
(M_{k},2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
It follows from
Proposition 5.15
that

\mathchoice{d_{k\alpha}\!\left(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g}\right)}{d_{k\alpha}(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g})}{d_{k\alpha}(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g})}{d_{k\alpha}(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g})}\leqslant e^{-\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}}\leqslant\eta/2.

Combined with our choice of
k
, we get
\mathchoice{d_{\infty}\!\left(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g}\right)}{d_{\infty}(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g})}{d_{\infty}(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g})}{d_{\infty}(c,c_{k,\varepsilon,g})}\leqslant\eta
, whence the result.
∎

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
Let
c\in\Lambda
.
For every
\eta\in(0,1)
, there is an open neighborhood
U\subset\partial X
of
c
such that for every
b\in U\cap\Lambda
, we have
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(b,c\right)}{d_{L}(b,c)}{d_{L}(b,c)}{d_{L}(b,c)}<\eta
.

Proof.

Fix
M>2L+503\alpha
.
Recall that
c\in\Lambda_{-}\cup\Lambda_{+}
.
According to
Proposition 4.13
, there is
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that
o
and
c
are
(M,2\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at some element
g\in G
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}>-\ln\eta
.
Let
x=g\gamma(-\varepsilon M)
and
x^{\prime}=g\gamma(\varepsilon M)
.
The set

U=\mathchoice{\left\{b\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle x,b\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}<3\alpha\right\}}{\{b\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle x,b\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}<3\alpha\}}{\{b\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle x,b\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}<3\alpha\}}{\{b\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle x,b\right\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}{\langle x,b\rangle_{x^{\prime}}}<3\alpha\}}.

is an open neighborhood of
c
.
By construction,
o
and
U
are
(M,3\alpha,\varepsilon)
-separated at
g
.
It follows from
Proposition 5.15
that
U\cap\Lambda
is contained in the ball of radius
\eta
centered at
c
with respect to the metric
d_{L}
.
∎

Proposition 5.24
.

Let
L>200\alpha
.
The distances
d_{\infty}
and
d_{L}
defines the same topology on
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
.
Moreover, it coincides with the quotient topology inherited from the subspace topology on
\Lambda\subset\partial X
.

Proof.

For simplicity, let us denote by
\mathcal{T}
the quotient topology on
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
.
Let
(z_{n})
be a sequence of
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
converging to
z\in\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
for
\mathcal{T}
.
After unwrapping all definitions one observes that
Lemma 5.23
implies that
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(z_{n},z\right)}{d_{L}(z_{n},z)}{d_{L}(z_{n},z)}{d_{L}(z_{n},z)}
converges to zero for every
L>200\alpha
.
We get by dominated convergence theorem that
\mathchoice{d_{\infty}\!\left(z_{n},z\right)}{d_{\infty}(z_{n},z)}{d_{\infty}(z_{n},z)}{d_{\infty}(z_{n},z)}
converges to zero.
Hence identity map
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,\mathcal{T})\to(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{\infty})
is continuous.

By construction the map
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{\infty})\to(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{L})
is Lipschitz.
Hence it suffices to prove that
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,d_{L})\to(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,\mathcal{T})
is continuous.
Let
(c_{n})
be a sequence of points in
\Lambda
and
c\in\Lambda
such that
\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{n},c\right)}{d_{L}(c_{n},c)}{d_{L}(c_{n},c)}{d_{L}(c_{n},c)}
converges to zero.
We denote by
c^{*}
an accumulation point of
c
for the topology on
\partial X
.
We claim that
c^{*}
is equivalent to
c
.
Let
R\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
.
There is
N\in\mathbf{N}
such that for every
n\geqslant N
, we have

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(c_{n},c\right)}{d_{L}(c_{n},c)}{d_{L}(c_{n},c)}{d_{L}(c_{n},c)}<e^{-(R+17\alpha)}.

In particular, there is
g\in C_{L}(o,c_{n})\cap C_{L}(o,c)
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}>R+17\alpha
.
Thus
(o,go)
as an
(\alpha,L-18\alpha)
-contracting tail (
Lemma 5.4
) while
c_{n}
and
c
belong to
\mathcal{O}_{o}(go,4\alpha)
(
Lemma 5.7
).
Consequently
\mathchoice{\left\|c_{n}-c\right\|_{K}}{\|c_{n}-c\|_{K}}{\|c_{n}-c\|_{K}}{\|c_{n}-c\|_{K}}\leqslant 20\alpha
, where
K
stands for the closed ball of radius
R
centered at
o
, see for instance
[
9
, Lemma 5.7]
.
This estimate holds for every
n\geqslant N
.
Passing to the limit we get
\mathchoice{\left\|c^{*}-c\right\|_{K}}{\|c^{*}-c\|_{K}}{\|c^{*}-c\|_{K}}{\|c^{*}-c\|_{K}}\leqslant 20\alpha
.
This computation does not depend on
R
, hence
\mathchoice{\left\|c^{*}-c\right\|_{\infty}}{\|c^{*}-c\|_{\infty}}{\|c^{*}-c\|_{\infty}}{\|c^{*}-c\|_{\infty}}\leqslant 20\alpha
, which completes our claim.
Since
\Lambda
is saturated,
c^{*}
belongs to
\Lambda
.
Thus
[c^{*}]=[c]
is an accumulation point of
[c_{n}]
for the quotient topology
\mathcal{T}
.
The above argument has two consequences.

(i)

Every subsequence of
[c_{n}]
admits an accumulation point (for
\mathcal{T}
).

(ii)

Every accumulation points of
[c_{n}]
(for
\mathcal{T}
) equals
[c]
.

Hence
[c_{n}]
converges to
[c]
in
(\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim,\mathcal{T})
.
∎

6
Geodesic flow

6.1
The spaces

We first define the spaces used to study the geodesic flow on
X
.
As previously,
X
is a proper, geodesic, metric space and
G
a group acting properly, by isometries on
X
with a contracting element.
We always assume that
G
is not virtually cyclic.

The space of geodesics.

Let
\mathcal{G}X
be the space of bi-infinite geodesics
\gamma\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
parametrized by arc length.
The action of
G
on
X
induces an action by translation of
G
on
\mathcal{G}X
.
The
geodesic flow

(\phi_{t})_{t\in\mathbf{R}}
on
\mathcal{G}X
is defined as follows:
\phi_{t}\gamma
is the path
s\mapsto\gamma(s+t)
.
One checks that the action of
G
commutes with the geodesic flow.
The
flip involution

\sigma\colon\mathcal{G}X\to\mathcal{G}X
sends the geodesic
\gamma
, to the geodesic
\bar{\gamma}
obtained by reversing the orientation, that is
\bar{\gamma}(s)=\gamma(-s)
, for every
s\in\mathbf{R}
.
The flip involution commutes with the action of
G
and anti-commutes with the flow, that is
\sigma\circ\phi_{t}=\phi_{-t}\circ\sigma
, for every
t\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
.

The space of the abstract geodesic flow.

Let us now define an abstract version of the unit tangent bundle of
X
.
We define the “diagonal”
\Delta
of
\partial X
as

\Delta=\mathchoice{\left\{(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial X\times\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\right\}}{\{(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial X\times\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\}}{\{(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial X\times\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\}}{\{(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial X\times\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\}}.

Definition 6.1
.

The
double boundary
of
X
is the set
\partial^{2}X=(\partial X\times\partial X)\setminus\Delta
.

Remark 6.1
.

Note that the definition does not depend on the base point
o\in X
.
Recall that the map
\partial X\times\partial X\to\mathbf{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}
sending
(c,c^{\prime})
to
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
is measurable.
Hence
\Delta
and
\partial^{2}X
are a Borel sets.
∎

We define on
\partial X\times\partial X
a
flip involution

\sigma
sending
(c,c^{\prime})
to
(c^{\prime},c)
.
It preserves
\partial^{2}X
.

Definition 6.2
.

The
space of the abstract geodesic flow
, denoted by
SX
is the product
\partial^{2}X\times\mathbf{R}
.

—

The
(abstract) geodesic flow

(\phi_{t})_{t\in\mathbf{R}}
on
SX
is defined as follows:
\phi_{t}
sends
(c,c^{\prime},s)
to
(c,c^{\prime},s+t)
.

—

The action of
G
on
\partial X
induces a diagonal action of
G
on
\partial^{2}X
.
This action extends to a
group action
of
G
on
SX
as follows:
given
g\in G
and
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
in
SX
we let

gv=\left(gc,gc^{\prime},s+\tau(g,v)\right),\quad\text{where}\quad\tau(g,v)=\frac{1}{2}\left[c^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,o)-c(g^{-1}o,o)\right].

—

The
flip involution

\sigma\colon SX\to SX
sends
(c,c^{\prime},s)
to
(c^{\prime},c,-s)
.

One checks that the action of
G
commutes with the geodesic flow and the flip.
The flip anti-commutes with the geodesic flow.
These actions are continuous.
The projection
SX\to\partial^{2}X
sending
(c,c^{\prime},s)
to
(c,c^{\prime})
is by construction equivariant for the action of
G
and the flip.

Toward a proper action.

We now relate the abstract space
SX
to the more concrete space of geodesics
\mathcal{G}X
.
Recall that any bi-infinite geodesic
\gamma\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
, defines two Busemann cocycles
c_{\gamma},c^{\prime}_{\gamma}\in\partial X
.
We define a map
\mathcal{G}X\to SX
by sending
\gamma
to
v=(c_{\gamma},c^{\prime}_{\gamma},s)
where

s=\frac{1}{2}\left[c^{\prime}_{\gamma}(o,\gamma(0))-c_{\gamma}(o,\gamma(0))\right].

It follows from the construction that the map
\mathcal{G}X\to SX
is equivariant for the actions of the group
G
, the flow and the flip.

If
X
is CAT(-1), then
\mathcal{G}X\to SX
is actually a homeomorphism.
When
X
is the universal cover of a geodesically complete negatively curved manifold,
\mathcal{G}X
can be seen as the unit tangent bundle of
X
(each unit vector defines a unique bi-infinite geodesic) and the map
\mathcal{G}X\to SX
is known as the Hopf coordinates.
Our study is motivated by this analogy.
In particular, we call
vectors
the elements of
SX
.
Given a vector
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
in
SX
, we call
c
( respectively
c^{\prime}
) the
past
( respectively
future
) of
v
.

Remark 6.2
.

If
X
is Gromov-hyperbolic, the map
\mathcal{G}X\to SX
is not necessarily onto (not every cocycle
c\in\partial X
is a Busemann cocycle given by a geodesic ray).
However it admits a coarse inverse, see
[
2
,
10
]
.
In our settings there is a priori no such coarse inverse.
If
X
is CAT(0), then
\mathcal{G}X\to SX
is not even coarsely injective.

Indeed consider the space
X=\mathbf{R}^{2}
endowed with the euclidean metric, so that
\partial X
is homeomorphic to
S^{1}
.
Fix
m\in\mathbf{R}
.
For every
s\in\mathbf{R}
, we write
\gamma_{s}\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
for the geodesic parametrized by

\gamma_{s}(t)=\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+m^{2}}}t-ms,\frac{m}{\sqrt{1+m^{2}}}t+s\right).

All these geodesics are parallel.
Moreover, their origins lie on a line orthogonal to
\gamma_{0}
.
Thus the image in
SX
of the set
\mathchoice{\left\{\gamma_{s}\;:\ s\in\mathbf{R}\right\}}{\{\gamma_{s}\;:\ s\in\mathbf{R}\}}{\{\gamma_{s}\;:\ s\in\mathbf{R}\}}{\{\gamma_{s}\;:\ s\in\mathbf{R}\}}
is reduced to a point.

This observation is the source of an other difficulty.
Consider the group
G=\mathbf{Z}^{2}
acting by integer translations on
X
.
Let
v_{0}
be the image of
\gamma_{0}
in
SX
.
If
m
is irrational, then the closure of
Gv_{0}
is
\mathchoice{\left\{\phi_{t}(v_{0})\;:\ t\in\mathbf{R}\right\}}{\{\phi_{t}(v_{0})\;:\ t\in\mathbf{R}\}}{\{\phi_{t}(v_{0})\;:\ t\in\mathbf{R}\}}{\{\phi_{t}(v_{0})\;:\ t\in\mathbf{R}\}}
.
Consequently the action of
G
on
SX
is not proper.
∎

The above remark motivates the following definitions.
A subset
U\subset\bar{X}\times\bar{X}
is
squeezing
if there exists a compact subset
K\subset X
such that for every pair
(c,c^{\prime})\in U
, any complete gradient arc from
c
to
c^{\prime}
intersects
K
.
The compact
K
is called the
bottleneck
of
U
.
According to
Proposition 3.6
, if
U
is an open squeezing set, then
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
is finite for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in U
.

Definition 6.3
.

Denote by
\partial_{0}^{2}X
the set of pairs
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial X\times\partial X
which admit a squeezing neighborhood in
\bar{X}\times\bar{X}
.
The subspace
S_{0}X
consists of all vectors
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
with
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial_{0}^{2}X
.

According to
Proposition 3.6
the map
SX\to\mathbf{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}
sending
(c,c^{\prime},s)
to
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
is continuous when restricted to
S_{0}X
.
In addition, it follows from the definition that
S_{0}X
is an open subset of
\partial X\times\partial X\times\mathbf{R}
.
In particular,
S_{0}X
is a Polish space.
It is also invariant under the actions of
G
, the flip map and the flow.

Proposition 6.4
.

The action of
G
on
S_{0}X
is properly discontinuous.

Proof.

Let
v_{1}=(c_{1},c^{\prime}_{1},s_{1})
and
v_{2}=(c_{2},c^{\prime}_{2},s_{2})
be two vectors in
S_{0}X
.
One can find a compact subset
K\subset X
and an open neighborhood
U_{i}\subset\bar{X}\times\bar{X}
of
(c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i})
such that for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in U_{i}
, any geodesic complete gradient arc from
c
to
c^{\prime}
crosses
K
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume that
K
contains the base point
o
.
We write
d
for its diameter.
Let
\eta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
.
We denote by
V_{\eta}(v_{i})\subset SX
the open neighborhood of
v_{i}
which consists of all vectors
w=(b,b^{\prime},t)
in
U_{i}\times\mathbf{R}
such that
\mathchoice{\left|s_{i}-t\right|}{|s_{i}-t|}{|s_{i}-t|}{|s_{i}-t|}<\eta
.
We claim that the set

S=\mathchoice{\left\{g\in G\;:\ gV_{\eta}(v_{1})\cap V_{\eta}(v_{2})\neq\emptyset\right\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ gV_{\eta}(v_{1})\cap V_{\eta}(v_{2})\neq\emptyset\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ gV_{\eta}(v_{1})\cap V_{\eta}(v_{2})\neq\emptyset\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ gV_{\eta}(v_{1})\cap V_{\eta}(v_{2})\neq\emptyset\}}

is finite.
Consider
g\in S
.
By definition there is
w=(b,b^{\prime},t)
in
V_{\eta}(v_{1})
such that
gw\in V_{\eta}(v_{2})
.
It follows from the triangle inequality that
\mathchoice{\left|\tau(g,w)\right|}{|\tau(g,w)|}{|\tau(g,w)|}{|\tau(g,w)|}<\mathchoice{\left|s_{2}-s_{1}\right|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}+2\eta
.

Let
\gamma
be a complete gradient line from
b
to
b^{\prime}
.
By definition of
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
, we can find two points
p
and
q
along
\gamma
such that
p\in K
and
q\in g^{-1}K
.
Recall that
o
belongs to
K
whose diameter is
d
.
In particular
\mathchoice{\left|\mathchoice{d\left(q,p\right)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}-\mathchoice{d\left(g^{-1}o,o\right)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}\right|}{|\mathchoice{d\left(q,p\right)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}-\mathchoice{d\left(g^{-1}o,o\right)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}|}{|\mathchoice{d\left(q,p\right)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}-\mathchoice{d\left(g^{-1}o,o\right)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}|}{|\mathchoice{d\left(q,p\right)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}-\mathchoice{d\left(g^{-1}o,o\right)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}{d(g^{-1}o,o)}|}\leqslant 2d
.
Moreover,
b
and
b^{\prime}
are
1
-Lipschitz, thus we get

\mathchoice{\left|\tau(g,w)\right|}{|\tau(g,w)|}{|\tau(g,w)|}{|\tau(g,w)|}\geqslant\mathchoice{\left|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,o)-b(g^{-1}o,o)\right]\right|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,o)-b(g^{-1}o,o)\right]|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,o)-b(g^{-1}o,o)\right]|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,o)-b(g^{-1}o,o)\right]|}\geqslant\mathchoice{\left|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]\right|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]|}-2d

However
p
and
q
lie on a complete gradient line from
b^{\prime}
to
b
.
Thus

\mathchoice{\left|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]\right|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]|}{|\frac{1}{2}\left[b^{\prime}(q,p)-b(q,p)\right]|}=\mathchoice{d\left(q,p\right)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}{d(q,p)}.

Recall that
\mathchoice{\left|\tau(g,w)\right|}{|\tau(g,w)|}{|\tau(g,w)|}{|\tau(g,w)|}<\mathchoice{\left|s_{2}-s_{1}\right|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}+2\eta
.
Combining our observations, we get
\mathchoice{d\left(o,go\right)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}{d(o,go)}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|s_{2}-s_{1}\right|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}+4d+2\eta
.
This shows that
S
is contained in
B_{G}(R)
where
R=\mathchoice{\left|s_{2}-s_{1}\right|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}+4d+2\eta
.
Since the action of
G
on
X
is proper, the latter set is finite, which complete the proof of our claim.
Given
\eta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
, any compact subset of
S_{0}X
can be covered by finitely many open subsets of the form
V_{\eta}(v)
, whence the result.
∎

6.2
The Bowen-Margulis measure

Given
x\in X
and
c,c^{\prime}\in\partial X
, we define the quantity

D_{x}(c,c^{\prime})=e^{-\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{x}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{x}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{x}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{x}}}.

If
X
is CAT(-1), then
D_{x}
is a visual metric on
\partial X
.
However we will not use this property here.
It follows from the definition that for every
c,c^{\prime}\in\partial X
, for every
x,y\in X
, and
g\in G
, we have

\displaystyle D_{x}(c,c^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\displaystyle e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left[c(x,y)+c^{\prime}(x,y)\right]}D_{y}(c,c^{\prime}),

(12)

\displaystyle D_{gx}(gc,gc^{\prime})

\displaystyle=D_{x}(c,c^{\prime}).

(13)

The first identity is a consequence of (
4
).
The second one follows from the fact that the action of
G
on
X
preserves Gromov products.

Bowen-Margulis current and measure.

As we did for the horoboundary, we endowed
\partial^{2}X
and
SX
with two a priori distinct
\sigma
-algebras.

—

We write
\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
( respectively
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
) for the product
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathfrak{B}
( respectively
\mathfrak{B}^{+}
) with itself.

—

We endow
SX
with the product
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
( respectively
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
) and the Borel
\sigma
-algebra on
\mathbf{R}
, which we denote by
\mathfrak{L}
( respectively
\mathfrak{L}^{+}
).

Note that
\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
and
\mathfrak{L}
are also the respective Borel
\sigma
-algebra of
\partial^{2}X
and
SX
.
Moreover
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
( respectively
\mathfrak{L}^{+}
) is a sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
( respectively
\mathfrak{L}
).

We fix once and for all
\omega\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
and a
G
-invariant,
\omega
-conformal density
\nu=(\nu_{x})
supported on
\partial X
.
Recall that
\omega\geqslant\omega_{G}
.
Moreover if
\nu_{o}
gives positive measure to the radial limit set, then the action of
G
on
X
is divergent and
\omega=\omega_{G}
(
Proposition 4.4
).

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that
\nu_{o}
gives positive measure to the radial limit set. Then
{\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o}}(\Delta)=0
.

Proof.

For every
c\in\partial X
, define the measurable set

\Delta_{c}=\mathchoice{\left\{c^{\prime}\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\right\}}{\{c^{\prime}\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\}}{\{c^{\prime}\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\}}{\{c^{\prime}\in\partial X\;:\ \mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}=\infty\}}.

It follows from Fubini theorem that

\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o}(\Delta)=\int\nu_{o}(\Delta_{c})d\nu_{o}.

Hence it suffices to prove that
\nu_{o}(\Delta_{c})=0
, for
\nu_{o}
-almost every
c\in\partial X
.

Since
\nu_{o}
gives positive measure to the radial limit set, it actually gives full measure to the contracting limit set (
Proposition 4.4
).
Let
c\in\Lambda_{\rm ctg}(G)
.
We are going to prove that
\Delta_{c}
is contained in the equivalence class
[c]
.
According to
[
9
, Proposition 5.9]
we can find
\alpha,r\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
and
L>r+32\alpha
such that
c
belongs to
\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}}(G,o,r,L)
.
In particular, there is a sequence of elements
(g_{n})
in
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}(L)
such that
(g_{n}o)
diverges to infinity and
c\in\mathcal{O}_{o}(g_{n}o,r)
, for every
n\in\mathbf{N}
.

Let
c^{\prime}\in\Delta_{c}
.
Let
n\in\mathbf{N}
.
Denote by
\tau_{n}
the
\alpha
-contracting tail associated to
g_{n}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
, there is a projection
p_{n}
of
o
on
\tau_{n}
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(g_{n}o,p_{n}\right)}{d(g_{n}o,p_{n})}{d(g_{n}o,p_{n})}{d(g_{n}o,p_{n})}\geqslant L
.
Denote by
q_{n}
and
q^{\prime}_{n}
respective projections of
c
and
c^{\prime}
on
\tau_{n}
.
According to
Lemma 3.15
,
\mathchoice{d\left(g_{n}o,q_{n}\right)}{d(g_{n}o,q_{n})}{d(g_{n}o,q_{n})}{d(g_{n}o,q_{n})}\leqslant r+7\alpha
.
We claim that
\mathchoice{d\left(g_{n}o,q^{\prime}_{n}\right)}{d(g_{n}o,q^{\prime}_{n})}{d(g_{n}o,q^{\prime}_{n})}{d(g_{n}o,q^{\prime}_{n})}\leqslant r+14\alpha
.
Assume on the contrary that it is not the case.
In particular
\mathchoice{d\left(q_{n},q^{\prime}_{n}\right)}{d(q_{n},q^{\prime}_{n})}{d(q_{n},q^{\prime}_{n})}{d(q_{n},q^{\prime}_{n})}>7\alpha
.
It follows from
Lemma 3.10
that
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{q_{n}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{q_{n}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{q_{n}}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{q_{n}}}\leqslant 5\alpha
.
In particular,
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}<\infty
.
This contradicts our assumption and completes the proof of our claim.
Note that
\mathchoice{d\left(p_{n},q^{\prime}_{n}\right)}{d(p_{n},q^{\prime}_{n})}{d(p_{n},q^{\prime}_{n})}{d(p_{n},q^{\prime}_{n})}>4\alpha
.
According to
Lemma 3.9
,
q^{\prime}_{n}
is
5\alpha
-close to any complete gradient line from
o
to
c^{\prime}
, hence
\mathchoice{\left\langle o,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{q^{\prime}_{n}}}{\langle o,c^{\prime}\rangle_{q^{\prime}_{n}}}{\langle o,c^{\prime}\rangle_{q^{\prime}_{n}}}{\langle o,c^{\prime}\rangle_{q^{\prime}_{n}}}\leqslant 5\alpha
.
It follows from the triangle inequality that
\mathchoice{\left\langle o,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g_{n}o}}{\langle o,c^{\prime}\rangle_{g_{n}o}}{\langle o,c^{\prime}\rangle_{g_{n}o}}{\langle o,c^{\prime}\rangle_{g_{n}o}}\leqslant r+19\alpha
.

We have proved that for every
n\in\mathbf{N}
, the cocycle
c^{\prime}
belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{o}(g_{n}o,r+19\alpha)
, as does
c
.
Since
\mathchoice{d\left(o,g_{n}o\right)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}
diverges to infinity, this forces that
\mathchoice{\left\|c-c^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}}{\|c-c^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{\|c-c^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{\|c-c^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}<20\alpha
, see
[
9
, Lemma 5.7]
, as we announced.
Recall that the measure
\nu_{o}
restricted to
\mathfrak{B}^{+}
has no atom (
Proposition 4.4
).
Hence
\nu_{o}(\Delta_{c})=\nu_{o}([c])=0
.
∎

Definition 6.6
.

The
Bowen-Margulis current
(associated to
\nu
), which we denote by
\mu
, is the measure on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B})
defined by

\mu=\frac{1}{D_{o}^{2\omega}}\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o}.

The
Bowen-Margulis measure
is the measure
m
on
(SX,\mathfrak{L})
defined by

m=\mu\otimes ds,

where
ds
stands for the Lebesgue measure on the factor
\mathbf{R}
of
SX
.

Lemma 6.7
.

The Bowen-Margulis current
\mu
is
G
-invariant, for the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B})
.
In particular, the measure
m
on
(SX,\mathfrak{L})
is invariant under the action of
G
as well as the flip map and the geodesic flow.
Moreover
\mu
and
m
are
\sigma
-finite measures on
\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
and
\mathfrak{L}
respectively.

Proof.

On the one hand, the measure

\frac{1}{D_{x}^{2\omega}}\nu_{x}\otimes\nu_{x}.

does not depend on the point
x\in X
.
This is indeed of a consequence of (
12
) together with the conformality of
\nu
.
On the other hand, the density
\nu
is
G
-invariant, while
D_{x}
is
G
-equivariant, see (
13
).
Consequently
\mu
is
G
-invariant.

By construction the map
D_{o}^{2\omega}\colon\partial^{2}X\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
is positive on
SX
.
In addition, it belongs to
L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\mu)
.
Thus
\mu
is
\sigma
-finite.
The Lebesgue measure on
\mathbf{R}
is
\sigma
-finite, hence so is
m
.
∎

Notations 6.1
.

For every
x,x^{\prime}\in X
, for every
r\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, we let

A(x,x^{\prime},r)=\mathcal{O}_{x^{\prime}}(x,r)\times\mathcal{O}_{x}(x^{\prime},r).

(14)

In plain words,
A(x,x^{\prime},r)\subset\bar{X}\times\bar{X}
is the set of pairs
(c,c^{\prime})
such that
c
and
c^{\prime}
are
r
-separated by
(x,x^{\prime})
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
h\in G
be a contracting element.
There are
r_{0}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
with the following property.
For every
r\geqslant r_{0}
, there is
N\in\mathbf{N}
such that for every integer
n\geqslant N
, the following holds:

—

A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
is squeezing;

—

0<\mu(A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)\cap\partial^{2}X)<\infty
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon,r_{0}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
be the parameters given by the Shadow Lemma (
Proposition 3.20
).
Since
h
is contracting, there is
\alpha\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that for every
n\in\mathbf{N}
, any geodesic from
h^{-n}o
to
h^{n}o
is
\alpha
-contracting.
Choose
r\geqslant r_{0}
.
We fix
N\in\mathbf{N}
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(o,h^{n}o\right)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}>2r+100\alpha
, for every
n\geqslant N
.
Let
n\geqslant N
.
According to
Proposition 3.18
,
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
is squeezing.
If
K
stands for the corresponding bottleneck, then for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
, there is
z\in K
such that
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{z}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{z}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{z}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{z}}=0
(
Proposition 3.6
).
Thus,
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
is uniformly bounded from above on
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)\cap\partial^{2}X
, which therefore has finite
\mu
-measure.
It remains to prove that
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
has positive measure.
It follows from the Shadow Lemma that

\nu_{o}\left(\mathcal{O}_{h^{n}o}(h^{-n}o,r)\right)\geqslant e^{-\omega\mathchoice{d\left(o,h^{n}o\right)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}}\nu_{h^{n}o}\left(\mathcal{O}_{h^{n}o}(h^{-n}o,r)\right)\geqslant\varepsilon e^{-3\omega\mathchoice{d\left(o,h^{n}o\right)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}}>0.

Similarly

\nu_{o}\left(\mathcal{O}_{h^{-n}o}(h^{n}o,r)\right)\geqslant\varepsilon e^{-3\omega\mathchoice{d\left(o,h^{n}o\right)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}{d(o,h^{n}o)}}>0,

whence the result.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

The set
S_{0}X
has positive
m
-measure.
If in addition,
\nu_{o}
gives full measure to the radial limit set, then
S_{0}X
contains a subset
B\in\mathfrak{L}^{+}
with full
m
-measure wich is flow, group and flip invariant.

Proof.

Let
h\in G
be a contracting element.
According to
Lemma 6.8
, there are
r,N\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that for every integer
n\geqslant N
, the set
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
defined in (
14
) is contained in
\partial^{2}X
and has positive
\mu
-measure.
It follows from
Proposition 3.18
, that if
n
is sufficiently large (compare to
r
) then
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)\times\mathbf{R}
is contained in
S_{0}X
.
Hence
S_{0}X
has positive
m
-measure.

Suppose now that
\nu_{o}
gives full measure to the radial limit set.
In particular,
\omega=\omega_{G}
and the action of
G
on
X
is divergent.
Denote by
\Lambda=\Lambda_{h}
the associated radial limit set studied in
Section 4.3
.
For simplicity we let
\Lambda^{(2)}=(\Lambda\times\Lambda)\cap\partial^{2}X
.
According to
Proposition 4.11
,
\Lambda
has full
\nu_{o}
-measure.
Recall that
\Lambda
is saturated.
Hence
B=\Lambda^{(2)}\times\mathbf{R}
is a subset in
\mathfrak{L}^{+}
with full
m
-measure.
By construction it is flow, group and flip invariant.
Combining Propositions
4.13
and
3.18
we observe that
B
is contained in
S_{0}X
, whence the result.
∎

6.3
Quotient space

Denote by
\mathfrak{L}_{G}
, ( respectively
\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
) the sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathfrak{L}
( respectively
\mathfrak{L}^{+}
) which consists of all
G
-invariant subsets of
\mathfrak{L}
( respectively
\mathfrak{L}^{+}
).
Define a
G
-invariant map
\xi\colon SX\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
as follows

\xi(v)=\left|G_{v}\right|^{-1},\quad\forall v\in SX,

(15)

where
G_{v}
is the set of elements in
G
fixing
v
.
As the group
G
is countable and acts by homeomorphisms on
SX
, the map
\xi
is measurable.
Since the action of
G
on
S_{0}X
is proper,
\xi
does not vanish on
S_{0}X
.
For the same reason, every
G
-orbit of a vector
v\in S_{0}X
is closed (for the induced topology on
S_{0}X
).
Thus we can find a measurable fondamental domain for the action of
G
on
S_{0}X
, that is a set
D\in\mathfrak{L}
, contained in
S_{0}X
, whose intersection with any
G
-orbit in
S_{0}X
contains exactly one point, see Bourbaki
[
4
, Chapitre IX, §6.9, Théorème 5]
.
We now endow
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G})
with a measure
m_{G}
defined as

m_{G}=\xi\mathbbnum{1}_{D}m.

The computation shows that

\mathbbnum{1}_{S_{0}X}=\xi\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{gD}.

Together with the fact that
m
is
G
-invariant, it implies that
m_{G}
does not depend on the choice of
D
.
It follows also from this relation that

m_{G}(GB)\leqslant m(B\cap S_{0}X)\leqslant m(B),\quad\forall B\in\mathfrak{L}.

Since
m
is a
\sigma
-finite measure on
\mathfrak{L}
, this implies that
m_{G}
is
\sigma
-finite on
\mathfrak{L}_{G}
as well.
Recall that
G
is countable.
Hence

m_{G}(B)=0\iff m(B\cap S_{0}X)=0,\quad\forall B\in\mathfrak{L}_{G}.

In particular,
m_{G}
is not the zero measure (
Lemma 6.9
).

One checks that if
\psi\colon SX\to SX
is homeomorphism commuting with
G
and preserving
S_{0}X
as well as
m
, then it preserves also
m_{G}
.
In particular,
m_{G}
is both flip and flow invariant.
Recall that
\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
is a sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathfrak{L}_{G}
.
We think of the action of
(\phi_{t})
on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
as the geodesic flow on the quotient
SX/G
.

Remark 6.3
.

By construction
m_{G}
is supported on
S_{0}X
.
We have to keep in mind though that
S_{0}X
does not a priori belong to
\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
.
However if the action of
G
on
X
is divergent, then
S_{0}X
contains a flow/flip invariant subset
B\in\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
with full
m
-, hence
m_{G}
-measure (
Lemma 6.9
).
∎

6.4
Conservativity of the geodesic flow

Conservative system.

We make here a little digression about conservative / dissipative systems.
Let
(\Omega,\mathfrak{A},\mu)
be a measure space.
In this paragraph, we denote by
(H,dh)
either a discrete countable group endowed with the counting measure, or
\mathbf{R}
endowed with the Lebesgue measure.
We assume that
H
acts on
(\Omega,\mathfrak{A})
preserving the measure class of
\mu
.
In particular, the action is mesurable in the sense that the map
H\times\Omega\to\Omega
sending
(h,\omega)
to
h\omega
is measurable.
A subset
W\subset\Omega
is called
wandering
if for
\mu
-almost every
\omega\in W
, we have

\int_{H}\mathbbnum{1}_{W}\left(h\omega\right)dh<\infty.

The action of
H
is
conservative
if there is no measurable wandering set with positive measure.
In this case, for every
A\in\mathfrak{A}
, for
\mu
-almost every
\omega\in A
,

\int_{H}\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(h\omega)dh=\infty.

The action of
H
is
dissipative
if
\Omega
is, up to measure zero, a countable union of wandering sets.

Lemma 6.10
.

Assume that the action of
H
preserves the measure
\mu
.
If there is summable map
f\colon\Omega\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that

\int_{H}f(h\omega)dh=\infty,\quad\mu\text{-a.e.},

then the action of
H
is conservative.

Proof.

Let
f_{1},f_{2}\colon\Omega\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
be two funtions.
Since
H
is unimodular and preserves the measure
\mu
, we have

\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{H}f_{1}\circ hdh\right)f_{2}d\mu=\int_{\Omega}f_{1}\left(\int_{H}f_{2}\circ hdh\right)d\mu.

Let
W\in\mathfrak{A}
be a wandering set.
We use the above relation with
f_{1}=f
and

f_{2}(\omega)=\frac{1}{c(\omega)}\mathbbnum{1}_{W}(\omega)\quad\text{where}\quad c(\omega)=\max\left\{1,\int_{H}\mathbbnum{1}_{W}\left(h\omega\right)dh\right\}

Since
W
is wandering
f_{2}(\omega)>0
for
\mu
-almost every
\omega\in W
.
Observe also that
c\colon\Omega\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
is
H
-invariant, thus

\int_{H}f_{2}(h\omega)dh\leqslant 1,\quad\forall\omega\in\Omega.

Consequently we get

\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{H}f_{1}\circ hdh\right)f_{2}d\mu\leqslant\int_{\Omega}f_{1}d\mu<\infty.

It follows from our assumption that
f_{2}
vanishes
\mu
-a.e., hence
\mu(W)=0
.
∎

If the measure
\mu
is
\sigma
-finite, then
\Omega
admits a
Hopf decomposition
, that is a partition
\Omega=\Omega_{C}\sqcup\Omega_{D}
which is
H
-invariant (up to measure zero) so that the action of
H
on
\Omega_{C}
( respectively
\Omega_{D}
) is conservative ( respectively dissipative)
[
14
]
.
This partition is unique up to measure zero.
The sets
\Omega_{C}
and
\Omega_{D}
are respectively called the
conservative
and
dissipative parts
of
\Omega
.

Application to the geodesic flow.

We now come back to the study of the geodesic flow.

Proposition 6.11
.

If
\nu_{o}
gives full measure to the radial limit set, then

(i)

the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial X\times\partial X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is conservative,

(ii)

the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative.

Remark 6.4
.

Recall that
\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
is a sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathfrak{L}_{G}
.
Hence if the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative, then so is the one on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
∎

Proof.

Let
h\in G
be a contracting element.
Denote by
\Lambda=\Lambda_{h}
the corresponding limit set studied in
Section 4.3
.
According to
Lemma 6.8
there is
r_{0}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
with the following property: for every
r\geqslant r_{0}
, there is
N(r)\in\mathbf{N}
, such that for every integer
n\geqslant N(r)
, the set
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
is strongly squeezing and its intersection with
\partial^{2}X
has positive, finite
\mu
-measure.

In view of
Proposition 4.13
, we can find
r\geqslant r_{0}
, such that the following holds: if
c\in\partial X
and
c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
are two non-equivalent cocycles, then for every
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, for every
n\in\mathbf{N}
, there is
u\in G
such that
c(o,uo)\geqslant T
and
(c,c^{\prime})\in uA(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
.
We now fix
n\geqslant N(r)
and let

A=A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)\cap\partial^{2}X

Since
A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r)
is squeezing, there is
\beta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in A
, we have
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}\leqslant\beta
.
Let
B=A\times\mathchoice{\left[-\beta\,,\beta\right]}{[-\beta\,,\beta]}{[-\beta\,,\beta]}{[-\beta\,,\beta]}
.
By construction,
A
belongs to
\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
and
GB\in\mathfrak{L}_{G}
.
Since
\mu(A)
is finite, so are
m(B)
and
m_{G}(GB)
.

Claim 6.12
.

Let
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
be a vector in
SX
.
If
c,c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
, then

\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(gc,gc^{\prime})=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt=\infty.

The value of the above integral is flow-invariant, hence we can assume that
s=-\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
.
Thus, for every
g\in G
, we have
g\phi_{t}(v)=(gc,gc^{\prime},t+\delta(g))
where

\delta(g)=-\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}+\tau(g,v)=-c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}.

Let
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
. According to our choice of
r
, there is
u\in G
such that
c^{\prime}(o,uo)\geqslant T
and
(c,c^{\prime})\in uA
.
In particular,
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{uo}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{uo}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{uo}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{uo}}\leqslant\beta
.
Hence,
u^{-1}\phi_{t}(v)
belongs to
B
, whenever

0\leqslant t-c^{\prime}(o,uo)\leqslant\beta.

Consequently,

\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(u^{-1}c,u^{-1}c^{\prime})=1\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{c^{\prime}(o,uo)}^{c^{\prime}(o,uo)+\beta}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt=\beta.

The construction holds for arbitrary
T
, hence
c^{\prime}(o,uo)
can be chosen arbitrarily large in the above identity.
Consequently,

\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(gc,gc^{\prime})=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt=\infty.

This proves our claim.

Assume now that
\nu_{o}
gives full measure to the radial limit set.
It forces that
\omega=\omega_{G}
.
Moreover the action of
G
on
X
is divergent.
Consequently, the set
\Lambda
has full
\nu_{0}
-measure (
Proposition 4.11
).
According to previous claim,

(i)

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(gc,gc^{\prime})=\infty
, for
(\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
-a.e.
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial^{2}X
.

(ii)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt=\infty
, for
m_{G}
-a.e.
v\in SX
.

Recall that the “diagonal”
\Delta=(\partial X\times\partial X)\setminus\partial^{2}X
has zero measure (
Lemma 6.5
).
Consequently,
(i)
holds for
(\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
-a.e. pair
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial X\times\partial X
.
The conclusion now follows from the conservativity criterion proved in
Lemma 6.10
.
∎

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
A
be a squeezing subset of
\partial^{2}X
and
B=A\times I
where
I\subset\mathbf{R}
is a bounded interval.
Let
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
be a vector in
SX
.
Assume that one of the following holds:

\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(gc,gc^{\prime})=\infty\quad\text{or}\quad\int_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi_{t}(v)=\infty.

Then
c
or
c^{\prime}
belong to the radial limit set
\Lambda_{\rm rad}(G)
of
G
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can suppose that
o
belongs to the bottleneck
K
of
A
.
We write
d
for its diameter.
Let
\nu\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
be a complete gradient line from
c
to
c^{\prime}
.
We choose our parametrization of
\nu
such that
c^{\prime}(o,x)=0
, where
x=\nu(0)
.
If

\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A}(gc,gc^{\prime})=\infty\quad\text{or}\quad\int_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi_{t}(v)=\infty,

then there is an infinite sequence
(g_{n})
of pairwise distinct elements of
G
such that for every
n\in\mathbf{N}
, the path
\nu
crosses
g_{n}K
, at the point
q_{n}=\nu(t_{n})
say.
Since the action of
G
on
X
is proper, the sequence
(g_{n}o)
diverges to infinity.
Recall that both
o
and
g_{n}^{-1}q_{n}
belong to
K
, whose diameter is bounded.
Thus, up to passing to a subsequence, we can assume that
(t_{n})
diverges to
\pm\infty
.
Assume that it diverges to
+\infty
.
Since
\nu
is a gradient line for
c^{\prime}
, and
c^{\prime}
is
1
-Lipschitz, we get

\displaystyle c^{\prime}(o,g_{n}o)\geqslant c^{\prime}(x,q_{n})-\mathchoice{d\left(q_{n},g_{n}o\right)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathchoice{d\left(x,q_{n}\right)}{d(x,q_{n})}{d(x,q_{n})}{d(x,q_{n})}-\mathchoice{d\left(q_{n},g_{n}o\right)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathchoice{d\left(o,g_{n}o\right)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}-[\mathchoice{d\left(o,x\right)}{d(o,x)}{d(o,x)}{d(o,x)}+2\mathchoice{d\left(q_{n},g_{n}o\right)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}{d(q_{n},g_{n}o)}]

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathchoice{d\left(o,g_{n}o\right)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}{d(o,g_{n}o)}-[\mathchoice{d\left(o,x\right)}{d(o,x)}{d(o,x)}{d(o,x)}+2d].

In other words, there is
R\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that
c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}_{o}(g_{n}o,R)
, for every sufficiently large
n\in\mathbf{N}
.
This implies that
c^{\prime}
belongs to the radial limit set.
It
(t_{n})
diverges to
-\infty
, we prove in the same way that
c
lies in the radial limit set.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

If
\nu_{o}
gives zero measure to the radial limit set, then

(i)

the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial_{0}^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is dissipative,

(ii)

the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is dissipative.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be the collection of open squeezing subsets of
\bar{X}\times\bar{X}
.
It follows from the definition that
\mathcal{U}
covers
\partial^{2}_{0}X
.
Recall that
\partial X
is compact metrizable, hence second-countable.
In particular, so is
\partial^{2}_{0}X
.
Consequently, it is covered by a countable subset
\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\mathcal{U}
.
We now write
\mathcal{B}_{0}
for the collection of all subets
B=(U\cap\partial^{2}X)\times\mathchoice{\left[s\,,s^{\prime}\right]}{[s\,,s^{\prime}]}{[s\,,s^{\prime}]}{[s\,,s^{\prime}]}
of
S_{0}X
where
U\in\mathcal{U}_{0}
and
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Q}
.
According to the above discussion,
\mathcal{B}_{0}
is a countable collection covering
S_{0}X
.

We now prove that the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is dissipative.
Denote by
\Omega_{C}
the conservative part of the geodesic flow.
Let
B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}
.
By recurrence, we know that for
m_{G}
-almost every vector
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
in
GB\cap\Omega_{C}
we have

\int_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbbnum{1}_{GB}\circ\phi(t)dt=\infty.

Hence for
m_{G}
-almost every vector
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
in
GB\cap\Omega_{C}
either
c\in\Lambda
or
c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
(
Lemma 6.13
).
However according to our assumption
\partial X\times\Lambda
and
\Lambda\times\partial X
have zero
(\nu_{0}\otimes\nu_{0})
-measure.
Hence
m_{G}(GB\cap\Omega_{C})=0
.
The sets
GB
as above are countably many and cover
S_{0}X
.
Moreover,
m_{G}
is supported on
S_{0}X
.
Consequently
\Omega_{C}
has zero
m_{G}
-measure.
The geodesic flow is therefore dissipative. The same argument as above shows that the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}_{0}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is dissipative.
∎

The next statement is a variation of the previous one for the saturated
\sigma
-algebras.
As we noticed earlier, if If
\nu_{o}
gives full measure to the radial limit, then the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative, hence so is the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
However
Proposition 6.14
does not automatically provide the converse statement.
This is purpose of the next result.

Proposition 6.15
.

If
\nu_{o}
gives zero measure to the radial limit set, then

(i)

the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is not conservative;

(ii)

the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is not conservative.

Proof.

Let
h\in G
be a contracting element.
Combining Lemmas
6.8
and
3.16
we can find
r_{0},r_{1}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
with
r_{0}\leqslant r_{1}
and
n\in\mathbf{N}
with the following properties.

—

The set
A_{i}=A(h^{-n}o,h^{n}o,r_{i})
is squeezing with finite positive measure.

—

The set

A^{+}=\mathcal{O}^{+}_{h^{n}o}(h^{-n}o,r_{0})\times\mathcal{O}^{+}_{h^{-n}o}(h^{n}o,r_{0})

belongs to
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
and satisfies
A_{0}\subset A^{+}\subset A_{1}
.

Let
S_{1}X=GA^{+}\times\mathbf{R}
.
We consider now the restriction of the geodesic flow to the space
(S_{1}X,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
By construction
S_{1}X
has positive
m_{G}
-measure.
Moreover
m_{G}
restricted to
(S_{1}X,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G})
is
\sigma
-finite.
In particular it admits a Hopf decomposition.
Denote by
\Omega_{C}
its conservative part.

Let
I
be a compact interval and
B=A^{+}\times I
.
As a subset of
A_{1}
, the set
A^{+}
is squeezing.
Proceeding as in the proof of
Proposition 6.14
, we prove that
m_{G}(GB\cap\Omega_{C})=0
.
However
S_{1}X
can be covered by countably many sets
GB
as above.
Therefore
m_{G}(\Omega_{C})=0
and the geodesic flow on
(S_{1}X,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is dissipative.
In particular,
S_{1}X
contains a wandering set
W\in\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
with positive measure.
Observe that
W
is also a wandering set for the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
Thus the latter is not conservative.

Proceeding in the same way, we prove first that the diagonal action of
G
on
(GA^{+},\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is dissipative, and then that the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is not conservative.
∎

6.5
Ergodicity of the geodesic flow

The remainder of this section is mainly dedicated to the proof of the following statement.

Theorem 6.16
.

Assume that the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative.
Then the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
is ergodic.

We assume that the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative.
It follows from
Proposition 6.15
that
\nu_{o}
gives positive measure to the radial limit set.
Hence
\omega=\omega_{G}
and the action of
G
on
X
is divergent (
Proposition 4.4
).
Following Bader and Furman
[
2
]
, we define the following two operations.

From the boundary to the unit tangent bundle.

We define a map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\mu)\times L^{1}(\mathbf{R},ds)&\to&L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L},m)\\
(f,\theta)&\mapsto&f_{\theta}\end{array}

(16)

where

f_{\theta}(v)=f(c,c^{\prime})\theta(s),\quad\text{for every $v=(c,c^{\prime},s)$ in $SX$}.

For every
f\in L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\mu)
and
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbf{R},ds)
, we have

\int_{SX}f_{\theta}dm=\int_{\partial^{2}X}fd\mu\int_{\mathbf{R}}\theta(s)ds.

Note that if
f
belongs to the subspace
L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
then
f_{\theta}
belongs to
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+},m)
.
If
\theta
is symmetric, then the operation
f\mapsto f_{\theta}
is equivariant with respect to the actions induced by the flip involutions.

From the cover to the quotient.

Given a
non-negative
summable function
f\in L^{1}_{+}(SX,\mathfrak{L},m)
, we define a
G
-invariant function

\begin{array}[]{rccc}\hat{f}\colon&SX&\to&\mathbf{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}\\
&v&\mapsto&\displaystyle\sum_{g\in G}f\left(gv\right).\end{array}

Recall that
\xi\colon SX\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
is the bounded,
G
-invariant map defined by (
15
) while
D\in\mathfrak{L}
stands for a Borel fundamental domain for the action of
G
on
S_{0}X
.
Moreover, we have the relation

\mathbbnum{1}_{S_{0}X}=\xi\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{gD}.

Combined with the fact that
m
is
G
-invariant, we get

\int_{SX}\hat{f}dm_{G}=\int\xi\left(\sum_{g\in G}f\circ g\right)\mathbbnum{1}_{D}dm=\int f\xi\left(\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{gD}\right)dm=\int f\mathbbnum{1}_{S_{0}X}dm.

(17)

In particular,
\hat{f}\in L^{1}_{+}(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
.

We can now extend this operation to any function in
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
(not just the non-negative ones).
Consider such a function
f
.
The above discussion applied to
h=\mathchoice{\left|f\right|}{|f|}{|f|}{|f|}
tells us that
\hat{h}
is summable, hence finite
m_{G}
-almost everywhere.
Thus the series

\sum_{g\in G}f\left(gv\right)

is absolutely convergent,
m_{G}
-almost everywhere, and can be used to define a function
\hat{f}
as above.
Observe also that

\mathchoice{\left|\hat{f}\right|}{|\hat{f}|}{|\hat{f}|}{|\hat{f}|}\leqslant\widehat{\mathchoice{\left|f\right|}{|f|}{|f|}{|f|}}.

so that
\hat{f}
belongs to
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
.
Note that this operation maps the subspace
L^{1}(S_{0}X,\mathfrak{L}^{+},m)
to
L^{1}(S_{0}X,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
Recall that the action of
G
commutes with the flip
\sigma
.
Hence the operation
f\mapsto\hat{f}
commutes with the action induced by the flip.

A particular space of functions.

For the remainder, we fix once and for all the function
\theta\colon\mathbf{R}\to\mathbf{R}
we are going to work with, namely

\theta(t)=e^{-2\mathchoice{\left|t\right|}{|t|}{|t|}{|t|}}.

For every
T_{1},T_{2}\in\mathbf{R}
with
T_{1}\leqslant T_{2}
we define

\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)=\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\theta(t+u)dt.

This function is « almost constant » on
[-T_{2},-T_{1}]
and decays exponentially outside this interval.
More precisely, we have the following useful estimates.

(i)

For every
u\in\mathbf{R}
,

\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{e^{2(T_{2}+u)},e^{-2(T_{1}+u)}\right\}.

(18)

(ii)

For every
u\in[-T_{2},-T_{1}]
,

\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)=1-\frac{1}{2}\left[e^{2(T_{1}+u)}+e^{-2(T_{2}+u)}\right].

Consequently, for every
u,u^{\prime}\in[-T_{2},-T_{1}]
,

\mathchoice{\left|\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)-\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u^{\prime})\right|}{|\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)-\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u^{\prime})|}{|\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)-\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u^{\prime})|}{|\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u)-\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}(u^{\prime})|}\leqslant\left[e^{\left(2T_{1}+u+u^{\prime}\right)}+e^{-\left(2T_{2}+u+u^{\prime}\right)}\right]\operatorname{sh}\left(\mathchoice{\left|u-u^{\prime}\right|}{|u-u^{\prime}|}{|u-u^{\prime}|}{|u-u^{\prime}|}\right).

(19)

Heuristically, the difference on the left hand side almost vanishes whenever
-T_{2}\ll u,u^{\prime}\ll-T_{1}
.

See
Figure 4
for a sketch of the graph of
\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}
.

Figure 4
:
Graph of the map
\Theta_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}
.

Denote by
\Lambda
the preferred limit set studied in
Section 5
.
The geodesic
\gamma\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
and the parameter
\alpha
are the same as the one fixed in this section.
Let
L>200\alpha
.
We consider here the
L
-visual pseudo-metric
d_{L}
on
\Lambda
introduced in
Definition 5.13
.
Let
b,b^{\prime}\in\Lambda
which are not equivalent.
We write
r_{1},r_{1}^{\prime},\beta
for the parameters given by
Lemma 5.18
applied with
b
and
b^{\prime}
.
Let
(r,r^{\prime})\in(0,r_{1})\times(0,r^{\prime}_{1})
.
We consider the function
f\colon\partial^{2}X\to\mathbf{R}
defined by
f=\mathbbnum{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}
, where
A
( respectively
A^{\prime}
) is the open ball in
(\Lambda,d_{L})
of radius
r
centered at
b
( respectively of radius
r^{\prime}
centered at
b^{\prime}
) seen as a subset of
\partial X
.
A function of this form is called
elementary
.
We denote by
\mathcal{E}
the collection of all elementary functions.
Note that
\mathcal{E}
is invariant under the action induced by
G
and the flip involution on
\partial^{2}X
.
We summarize in the next statement the properties of elementary functions.

Lemma 6.17
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{E}
.
There is
\beta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that the following holds:

(i)

The map
f
belongs to
L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
.

(ii)

The map
\hat{f}_{\theta}
is bounded.

(iii)

Let
c_{1},c_{2},c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
(c_{1},c^{\prime})
and
(c_{2},c^{\prime})
belongs to
\partial^{2}X
.
There is
T\in\mathbf{R}
, such that

f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})=f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime}),\quad\text{whenever}\quad c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant T.

(iv)

For every
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial^{2}X
.
If
f(c,c^{\prime})\neq 0
, then
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}\leqslant\beta
.

(v)

For every
a\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, there is
N\in\mathbf{N}
such that for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial^{2}X
, for every
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
the set

\mathchoice{\left\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\right\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\}}

contains at most
N
elements.

Proof.

Let
b,b^{\prime}\in\Lambda
which are not equivalent.
We write
r_{1},r_{1}^{\prime},\beta
for the parameters given by
Lemma 5.18
applied with
b
and
b^{\prime}
.
Let
(r,r^{\prime})\in(0,r_{1})\times(0,r^{\prime}_{1})
.
Denote by
A
( respectively
A^{\prime}
) the open ball of
(\Lambda,d_{L})
of radius
r
( respectively
r^{\prime}
) and consider the elementary function
f=\mathbbnum{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}
.
It follows from
Lemma 5.18
, that the map
\partial^{2}X\to\mathbf{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}
sending
(c,c^{\prime})
to
\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
is bounded above by
\beta
, when restricted to
A\times A^{\prime}
, whence
(iv)
.
Consequently
f
belongs to
L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
, which proves
(i)
.

Let
c_{1},c_{2},c^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
(c_{1},c^{\prime})
and
(c_{2},c^{\prime})
belongs to
\partial^{2}X
.
According to
Lemma 5.17
, there is
T\in\mathbf{R}
such that for every
g\in G
,

\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(gc_{1},gc_{2}\right)}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}{d_{L}(gc_{1},gc_{2})}<r,\quad\text{provided}\quad c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant T.

Consider such an element
g
.
Recall that
d_{L}
is an ultra-metric.
Hence
gc_{1}\in A
if and only if
gc_{2}\in A
.
Thus
f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})=f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime})
, whence
