(iii)
.

Recall that the action of
G
on
X
is proper.
Hence there is a map
N\colon\mathbf{R}_{+}\to\mathbf{N}
, such that for every
\ell\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
and
y\in X
, the set

F(y,\ell)=\mathchoice{\left\{g\in G\;:\ \mathchoice{d\left(y,go\right)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}\leqslant\ell\right\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ \mathchoice{d\left(y,go\right)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}\leqslant\ell\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ \mathchoice{d\left(y,go\right)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}\leqslant\ell\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ \mathchoice{d\left(y,go\right)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}{d(y,go)}\leqslant\ell\}}

contains at most
N(\ell)
elements.
Let
(c,c^{\prime})\in\partial^{2}X
such that
f(c,c^{\prime})\neq 0
.
Fix a complete gradient line
\nu\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
from
c
to
c^{\prime}
parametrized such that
c^{\prime}(o,\nu(0))=0
.
Consider an element
g\in G
such that
f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0
, i.e.
(gc,gc^{\prime})\in A\times A^{\prime}
.
It follows from
Lemma 5.18
that there is
y=\nu(t)
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(y,g^{-1}o\right)}{d(y,g^{-1}o)}{d(y,g^{-1}o)}{d(y,g^{-1}o)}\leqslant\beta
.
According to our choice of parametrization, we have

c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-t=c^{\prime}(\nu(t),\nu(0))+c^{\prime}(\nu(0),o)+c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)=c^{\prime}(y,g^{-1}o).

As
c^{\prime}
is
1
-Lipschitz, we get
\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-t\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-t|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-t|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-t|}\leqslant\beta
.
It follows from this discussion that for every
a,T\in\mathbf{R}
, the set

\mathchoice{\left\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\right\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\}}{\{g\in G\;:\ f(gc,gc^{\prime})\neq 0,\mathchoice{\left|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T\right|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}{|c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-T|}\leqslant a\}}\subset F\left(\nu(T),a+2\beta\right),

which completes the proof of
(v)
.

Let are left to prove that
\hat{f}_{\theta}
is bounded.
Let
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
be a vector of
SX
.
Observe that if
(c,c^{\prime})\notin\Lambda\times\Lambda
, then
\hat{f}_{\theta}(v)=0
.
Assume now that
(c,c^{\prime})\in\Lambda\times\Lambda
.
In particular, there exists a bi-infinite gradient line
\nu\colon\mathbf{R}\to X
from
c
to
c^{\prime}
(Propositions
4.13
and
3.18
).
We choose the parametrization of
\nu
such that

\frac{1}{2}\left[c^{\prime}(o,x)-c(o,x)\right]=s,\quad\text{where}\quad x=\nu(0).

The computation yields,

\hat{f}_{\theta}(v)=\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}(gc,gc^{\prime})\theta\left(s+\tau(g,v)\right).

Consider an element
g\in G
such that the corresponding term in the above sum does not vanish, i.e.
(gc,gc^{\prime})\in A\times A^{\prime}
.
It follows from
Lemma 5.18
that there is
y=\nu(t)
such that
\mathchoice{d\left(y,g^{-1}o\right)}{d(y,g^{-1}o)}{d(y,g^{-1}o)}{d(y,g^{-1}o)}\leqslant\beta
.
According to our choice of parametrization of
\nu
we have

s+\tau(g,v)=\frac{1}{2}\left[c^{\prime}(g^{-1}o,x)-c(g^{-1}o,x)\right].

Since
c
and
c^{\prime}
are
1
-Lipschitz, this quantity differs from

\frac{1}{2}\left[c^{\prime}(y,x)-c(y,x)\right]=-t=\pm\mathchoice{d\left(x,y\right)}{d(x,y)}{d(x,y)}{d(x,y)}.

by at most
\beta
.
Combined with the triangle inequality, it yields

\mathchoice{\left|s+\tau(g,v)\right|}{|s+\tau(g,v)|}{|s+\tau(g,v)|}{|s+\tau(g,v)|}\geqslant\mathchoice{d\left(x,g^{-1}o\right)}{d(x,g^{-1}o)}{d(x,g^{-1}o)}{d(x,g^{-1}o)}-2\beta.

Coming back the computation of
\hat{f}_{\theta}
, the above discussion implies that

\hat{f}_{\theta}(v)\leqslant e^{4\beta}\sum_{g\in E}e^{-2\mathchoice{d\left(x,go\right)}{d(x,go)}{d(x,go)}{d(x,go)}}

where
E
consists of all elements
g\in G
such that
go
is
\beta
-close to
\nu
.
Hence

\hat{f}_{\theta}(v)\leqslant e^{4\beta}\sum_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}\sum_{g\in F(\nu(k\beta),2\beta)}e^{-2\mathchoice{d\left(x,go\right)}{d(x,go)}{d(x,go)}{d(x,go)}}\leqslant N(2\beta)e^{8\beta}\sum_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}e^{-2\mathchoice{\left|k\right|}{|k|}{|k|}{|k|}\beta}<\infty.

The above estimate does not depend on
v
, hence
\hat{f}_{\theta}
is bounded.
∎

Proposition 6.18
.

The linear space spanned by
\mathcal{E}
is dense in
L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
.

Proof.

Simple functions are dense in
L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
.
Thus we just have to prove any function of the form
\mathbbnum{1}_{B}
with
B\in\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
can be approximated by a linear combination of elements of
\mathcal{E}
, provided
\mu(B)<\infty
.
However
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
is a product
\sigma
-algebra, thus it suffices to consider subsets
B\in\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
of the form
B=A\times A^{\prime}
with
A,A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}^{+}
.
Since
\Lambda
has full
\nu_{o}
-measure, we can even assume that
A
and
A^{\prime}
are contained in
\Lambda
.
Recall that
\Lambda/\negthickspace\sim
endowed with the Borel
\sigma
-algebra induced by
d_{L}
is isomorphic as measurable space to
(\Lambda,\mathfrak{B}^{+})
.
A Vitali type argument shows that any subset
A\in\mathfrak{B}^{+}
can be approximated by a finite disjoint union of arbitrarily small balls for the distance
d_{L}
, whence the result.
∎

Lemma 6.19
.

There exists
\rho\in\mathcal{E}
, such that

—

\hat{\rho}_{\theta}
is positive
m_{G}
-almost everywhere;

—

the map
P\colon\partial^{2}X\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
given by

P(c,c^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}(c,c^{\prime},t)\theta(t)dt,

is bounded and positive
\mu
-almost everywhere.

Remark 6.5
.

Note that the existence of
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}
implies that the measure
m_{G}
on
\mathfrak{L}_{G}^{+}
is
\sigma
-finite.
∎

Proof.

Recall that
c_{\gamma}
and
c^{\prime}_{\gamma}
are the Busemann cocycles associated to the path
\gamma
as in
Example 3.1
.
Note that
c_{\gamma}
and
c^{\prime}_{\gamma}
are not equivalent.
We write
r_{1},r_{1}^{\prime},\beta
for the parameters given by
Lemma 5.18
applied with
c_{\gamma}
and
c^{\prime}_{\gamma}
.
Let
(r,r^{\prime})\in(0,r_{1})\times(0,r^{\prime}_{1})
.
Let
A
( respectively
A^{\prime}
) be the ball in
(\Lambda,d_{L})
of radius
r
( respectively
r^{\prime}
) centered at
c_{\gamma}
( respectively
c^{\prime}_{\gamma}
) seen as a subset of
\partial X
.
We choose
\rho=\mathbbnum{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}
, which is elementary by construction.
In particular
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}
is bounded, hence so is
P
.

We now prove that
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}
and
P
are positive almost everywhere.
Recall that
\Lambda
decomposes as a union
\Lambda=\Lambda_{-}\cup\Lambda_{+}
where
\Lambda_{-}
and
\Lambda_{+}
are two
G
-invariant, saturated Borel sets with full
\nu_{o}
-measure (see the beginning of
Section 5.1
).
Thus it suffices to show that
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}(c,c^{\prime},s)>0
and
P(c,c^{\prime})>0
, whenever
(c,c^{\prime})\in\Lambda\times\Lambda_{+}
.
Consider a vector
v=(c,c^{\prime},s)
for some
(c,c^{\prime})\in\Lambda\times\Lambda_{+}
and
s\in\mathbf{R}
.
According to
Lemma 5.16
, there is
u\in G
such that

\max\left\{\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(uc,c_{\gamma}\right)}{d_{L}(uc,c_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(uc,c_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(uc,c_{\gamma})},\mathchoice{d_{L}\!\left(uc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma}\right)}{d_{L}(uc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(uc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{d_{L}(uc^{\prime},c^{\prime}_{\gamma})}\right\}<\min\{r,r^{\prime}\}.

Hence
(uc,uc^{\prime})\in A\times A^{\prime}
.
Consequently

\hat{\rho}_{\theta}(v)\geqslant\rho_{\theta}(uv)\geqslant\theta(s+\tau(u,v))>0.

Moreover

\displaystyle P(c,c^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geqslant\sum_{g\in G}\mathbbnum{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}(gc,gc^{\prime})\int_{\mathbf{R}}\theta\left(t+\tau(g,v)\right)\theta(t)dt

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{\mathbf{R}}\theta\left(t+\tau(u,v)\right)\theta(t)dt>0.\qed

Running the Hopf argument.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{E}
.
There is a flow/flip invariant subset
Y\in\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
with full
m_{G}
-measure, such that for all vectors
v_{1},v_{2}\in Y
of the form
v_{i}=(c_{i},c^{\prime},s_{i})
(i.e. with the same futur) the map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbf{R}_{+}&\to&\mathbf{R}_{+}\\
T&\mapsto&\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{2})\right]dt\end{array}

is bounded.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda^{(2)}=(\Lambda\times\Lambda)\cap\partial^{2}X
.
The set
Y=\Lambda^{(2)}\times\mathbf{R}
has full
m_{G}
-measure.
It is invariant by
G
, the flow, and the flip.
Consider now
v_{1}=(c_{1},c^{\prime},s_{1})
and
v_{2}=(c_{2},c^{\prime},s_{2})
in
Y
.
According to
Lemma 6.17
,
\hat{f}_{\theta}
is bounded.
Consequently, for every
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbf{R}
, the map sending
T\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
to

\mathchoice{\left|\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{2})\right]dt-\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{1}}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{2}}(v_{2})\right]dt\right|}{|\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{2})\right]dt-\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{1}}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{2}}(v_{2})\right]dt|}{|\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{2})\right]dt-\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{1}}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{2}}(v_{2})\right]dt|}{|\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v_{2})\right]dt-\int_{0}^{T}\left[\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{1}}(v_{1})-\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t+t_{2}}(v_{2})\right]dt|}

is bounded.
Therefore, we can advantageously replace
v_{i}
by
w_{i}=\phi_{t_{i}}(v_{i})
for a suitable value of
t_{i}
.
In practice, we take
t_{i}=-s_{i}-\mathchoice{\left\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}
.
For simplicity we let

F_{i}(T)=\int_{0}^{T}\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(w_{i})dt,\quad\forall T\geqslant 0.

The computation yields

F_{i}(T)=\sum_{g\in G}f(gc_{i},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{i}(g)),\quad\text{where}\quad\delta_{i}(g)=-c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)-\mathchoice{\left\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}.

Before starting our estimations, we define some other auxiliary objets / quantities.
It follows from
Lemma 6.17
that there is
\beta,T_{0}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
such that for every
g\in G
, we have

f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})=f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime}),\quad\text{whenever}\quad c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant T_{0}.

Moreover
\mathchoice{\left\langle b,b^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}\leqslant\beta
, as soon as
f(b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
.
We split the group
G
as follows: for every
k\in\mathbf{Z}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, we write
G_{i,k}
for the set of all
g\in G
, such that

—

k\beta\leqslant c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)<(k+1)\beta
, and

—

f(gc_{i},gc^{\prime})\neq 0
.

According to
Lemma 6.17
, the cardinality of
G_{i,k}
is bounded above by a number
N
that does not depend on
k
nor on
i
.
In addition, we set
G_{k}=G_{1,k}\cup G_{2,k}
.

We now decompose the work as follows.

\mathchoice{\left|F_{1}(T)-F_{2}(T)\right|}{|F_{1}(T)-F_{2}(T)|}{|F_{1}(T)-F_{2}(T)|}{|F_{1}(T)-F_{2}(T)|}\leqslant\sum_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}S_{k},

where

S_{k}=\sum_{g\in G_{k}}\mathchoice{\left|f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))\right|}{|f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))|}{|f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))|}{|f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime})\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))|}.

We are going to estimate each
S_{k}
depending on the value of
k
.

Claim 6.21

(Estimate for the tails)
.

For every integer
k\in\mathbf{Z}
,

S_{k}\leqslant Ne^{4\beta}\min\left\{e^{2(T-k\beta)},e^{2k\beta}\right\}.

Let
k\in\mathbf{Z}
.
A (brutal) upper bound for
S_{k}
using (
18
) is

S_{k}\leqslant\sum_{i\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{g\in G_{i,k}}\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{i}(g))\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{g\in G_{i,k}}\min\left\{e^{2T+2\delta_{i}(g)},e^{-2\delta_{i}(g)}\right\}.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Let
g\in G_{i,k}
.
Since
f(gc_{i},gc^{\prime})\neq 0
, we know from
Lemma 6.17
that
\mathchoice{\left\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}\leqslant\beta
, hence

-(k+2)\beta<\delta_{i}(g)\leqslant-k\beta.

Recall also that
G_{i,k}
contains at most
N
elements.
Our claim now follows from a direct computation.

Claim 6.22

(Estimate for the center)
.

Let
k\in\mathbf{Z}
.
If
T_{0}\leqslant k\beta\leqslant T-2\beta
, then

S_{k}\leqslant Ne^{4\beta}\operatorname{sh}(\beta)\left[e^{-2k\beta}+e^{-2(T-k\beta)}\right]

Let
g\in G_{k}
.
According to our hypothesis,
c^{\prime}(o,g^{-1}o)\geqslant T_{0}
.
Consequently
f(gc_{1},gc^{\prime})=f(gc_{2},gc^{\prime})
.
In other words
G_{1,k}=G_{2,k}=G_{k}
.
Thus,

S_{k}=\sum_{g\in G_{k}}\mathchoice{\left|\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))\right|}{|\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))|}{|\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))|}{|\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{1}(g))-\Theta_{0}^{T}(\delta_{2}(g))|}.

Reasoning as in our previous claim, we observe that
\mathchoice{\left\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}{\langle c_{i},c^{\prime}\rangle_{g^{-1}o}}\leqslant\beta
.
Consequently

-T\leqslant-(k+2)\beta<\delta_{i}(g)\leqslant-k\beta\leqslant-T_{0}\leqslant 0.

Moreover
\mathchoice{\left|\delta_{1}(g)-\delta_{2}(g)\right|}{|\delta_{1}(g)-\delta_{2}(g)|}{|\delta_{1}(g)-\delta_{2}(g)|}{|\delta_{1}(g)-\delta_{2}(g)|}\leqslant\beta
.
Recall that
G_{k}
contains at most
N
elements.
The claim now follows from a direct computation using (
19
).

Set
C=Ne^{4\beta}\max\{1,\operatorname{sh}\beta\}
.
Combining our estimates and reindexing the sums, we get

—

\displaystyle\sum_{k\beta<T_{0}}S_{k}\leqslant C\sum_{k\beta<T_{0}}e^{2k\beta}\leqslant C\sum_{k>-T_{0}/\beta}e^{-2k\beta},

—

\displaystyle\sum_{T_{0}\leqslant k\beta\leqslant T-2\beta}S_{k}\leqslant C\sum_{T_{0}\leqslant k\beta\leqslant T-2\beta}\left(e^{-2k\beta}+e^{-2(T-k\beta)}\right)\leqslant 2C\sum_{k\geqslant 0}e^{-2k\beta},

—

\displaystyle\sum_{k\beta>T-2\beta}S_{k}\leqslant C\sum_{k\beta>T-2\beta}e^{2(T-k\beta)}\leqslant C\sum_{k\geqslant-2}e^{-2k\beta}.

Observe that all these upper bounds are finite and do not depend on
T
.
Hence the result is proven.
∎

We now fix a function
\rho
as given by
Lemma 6.19
.
Up to multiplying
\rho
by a positive scalar, we may assume that

\int_{SX}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}dm_{G}=\int_{S_{0}X}\rho_{\theta}dm=\int_{\partial^{2}X}\rho d\mu=1.

As in
Lemma 6.19
, we write
P\colon\partial^{2}X\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
for the map defined by

P(c,c^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}(c,c^{\prime},t)\theta(t)dt.

It is bounded and positive
\mu
-almost everywhere.

Proposition 6.23
.

For every function
f\in L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
, for almost vector every
v\in(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
we have

\lim_{T\to\pm\infty}\frac{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt}=\int_{\partial^{2}X}fPd\mu

Proof.

On the one hand,
\hat{f}_{\theta}\colon SX\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
belongs to
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
On the other hand, the function
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}\in L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is positive almost everywhere.
Since the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative, the Hopf ergodic theorem
[
14
]
tells us that for almost every vector
v\in(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
,

\lim_{T\to\pm\infty}\frac{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt}=f_{\infty}(v),\quad\text{where}\quad f_{\infty}=\mathbbalpha{E}_{\hat{\rho}_{\theta}m_{G}}\left(\hat{f}_{\theta}\middle|\mathfrak{I}^{+}\right)

(20)

is the conditional expectation of
\hat{f}_{\theta}
with respect to the sub-
\sigma
-algebra
\mathfrak{I}^{+}
which consists of all flow-invariant subsets of
\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
and the measure
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}m_{G}
.

Suppose now that
f
belongs to
\mathcal{E}
.
Denote by
Y\in\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G}
the flow/flip invariant subset given by
Proposition 6.20
.
Let
v_{1}=(c_{1},c^{\prime},s_{1})
and
v_{2}=(c_{2},c^{\prime},s_{2})
be two vectors in
Y
with the same futur.
It follows from
Proposition 6.20
that
f_{\infty}(v_{1})=f_{\infty}(v_{2})
.
In other words
f_{\infty}
only depends on the future
m_{G}
-almost surely.
Recall that the flow anti-commutes with the flip involution
\sigma
.
Applying the same argument with
f\circ\sigma
, which belongs to
\mathcal{E}
as well, we obtain that
f_{\infty}
only depends on the past
m_{G}
-almost surely.
As
\mu
is equivalent to a product measure, one proves using Fubini’s theorem that
f_{\infty}
is constant
m_{G}
-almost everywhere.
By definition of the conditional expectation, the almost sure value
M
of
f_{\infty}
satisfies

M=\int_{SX}f_{\infty}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}dm_{G}=\int_{SX}\hat{f}_{\theta}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}dm_{G}.

Since
\hat{\rho}_{\theta}
is
G
-invariant, the previous inequality yields

M=\int_{SX}\hat{f}_{\theta}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}dm_{G}=\int_{SX}\widehat{f_{\theta}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}}dm_{G}=\int_{S_{0}X}f_{\theta}\hat{\rho}_{\theta}dm=\int_{\partial^{2}X}fPd\mu.

Since
P
is bounded, both maps

f\mapsto\mathbbalpha{E}_{\hat{\rho}_{\theta}m_{G}}\left(\hat{f}_{\theta}\middle|\mathfrak{I}^{+}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad f\mapsto\int_{\partial^{2}X}fPd\mu

are countinuous linear maps on
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
The above discussion shows that they agree on
\mathcal{E}
.
According to
Proposition 6.18
, the linear space spanned by
\mathcal{E}
is dense in
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
.
Hence the two above functions agree on
L^{1}(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Although we followed the classical Hopf argument, we have not quite proved yet that the geodesic flow is ergodic.
Indeed our strategy applies only to functions of the form
\hat{f}_{\theta}
for some
f\in L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
.
Nevertheless this is enough to show that the diagonal action of
G
on the double boundary is ergodic.
We first need an auxiliary lemma, telling us that the measure
\mu
restricted to
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
is
\sigma
-finite.

Lemma 6.24
.

Consider the map
\psi\colon\partial^{2}X\to\mathbf{R}^{*}_{+}
given by

\psi(c,c^{\prime})=\sup e^{-2\omega\mathchoice{\left\langle b,b^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}}.

where the supremum runs over all pairs
(b,b^{\prime})\in\partial^{2}X
such that
b\sim c
, and
b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}
.
Then
\psi\in L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
.

Proof.

By construction this is a
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
-measurable function.
Recall that
\Lambda
has full
\nu_{o}
-measure.
Moreover for every
b,c\in\Lambda
in the same equivalence class, we have
\mathchoice{\left\|b-c\right\|_{\infty}}{\|b-c\|_{\infty}}{\|b-c\|_{\infty}}{\|b-c\|_{\infty}}\leqslant 20\alpha
.
See for instance
[
9
, Proposition 5.10]
.
Consequently,
\mu
-almost everywhere, we have

\psi(c,c^{\prime})\leqslant e^{40\omega\alpha}e^{-2\omega\mathchoice{\left\langle c,c^{\prime}\right\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}{\langle c,c^{\prime}\rangle_{o}}}\leqslant e^{40\omega\alpha}D^{2\omega}_{o}(c,c^{\prime}).

Hence
\psi
is summable.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 6.16
.

Let
B\in\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
be a
G
-invariant subset of
\partial^{2}X
with positive
\mu
-measure.
Fix a positive function
\psi\in L^{1}(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\mu)
as given by
Lemma 6.24
.
Applying
Proposition 6.23
with
f=\mathbbnum{1}_{B}\psi
, there is a subset
A\subset\partial^{2}X
in
\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+}
with full
\mu
-measure, such that for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in A
, for every sufficiently large
T
we have

\int_{0}^{T}\hat{f}_{\theta}\circ\phi_{t}(v)dt>0,\quad\text{where}\quad v=(c,c^{\prime},0).

Hence for every
(c,c^{\prime})\in A
, there is
g\in G
, such that
f(gc,gc^{\prime})>0
, that is
(c,c^{\prime})\in g^{-1}B
.
Since
B
is
G
-invariant,
A\subset B
, thus
B
has full
\mu
-measure.
∎

We complete this section with the following statement that would be useful to prove the converse of
Theorem 6.16
.
In particular, we do not assume anymore that the geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative.

Proposition 6.25
.

Assume that the diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is ergodic, then
(\partial X,\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o})
has no atom.

Proof.

Assume on the contrary that
(\partial X,\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o})
has an atom, say
A
.
Let
g
be contracting element and
Y
an axis of
g
.
Since
\nu
is
G
-invariant,
hA
is an atom for every
h\in G
.
According to
Lemma 3.21
, there is
D\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that for every
h\in G
, one can find an atom
B_{h}\subset A
such that
hB_{h}\cap\partial^{+}Y
is empty and the projection of
hB_{h}
on
Y
has diameter at most
D
.
We let

B=\bigcap_{h\in G}B_{h}.

Since
G
is countable, it is an atom.
Moreover for every
h\in G
, the projection of
hB
on
Y
has diameter at most
D
.
In particular, we can find
g_{1}\in\left\langle g\right\rangle
such that the projections of
B
and
g_{1}B
on
Y
are sufficiently far so that
B\times g_{1}B
is contained in
\partial^{2}X
(see
Lemma 3.10
).
According to
Lemma 3.22
,
\mathchoice{\operatorname{Stab}\left(B\right)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}
does not contain a contracting element.
It follows from
Proposition 3.12
that the projection on
Y
of

Z=\bigcup_{h\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Stab}\left(B\right)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}}hg_{1}B

is bounded.
Consequently we can find
g_{2}\in\left\langle g\right\rangle
such that
\pi_{Y}(Z)
and
\pi_{Y}(g_{2}B)
are disjoint while
B\times g_{2}B
is contained in
\partial^{2}X
.

As
B
is an atom both
B\times g_{1}B
and
B\times g_{2}B
have positive measure.
By ergodicity
G(B\times g_{1}B)
has full measure.
Since
G
is countable, there is
h\in G
such that
h(B\times g_{1}B)\cap(B\times g_{2}B)
has positive measure.
In particular
\nu_{o}(B\cap hB)>0
while
hg_{1}B\cap g_{2}B
is non-empty.
The former says that
h
belongs to
\mathchoice{\operatorname{Stab}\left(B\right)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}{\operatorname{Stab}(B)}
.
Hence the condition
hg_{1}B\cap g_{2}B\neq\emptyset
contradicts our choice of
g_{2}
.
∎

6.6
The Hopf-Tsuji-Sullivan dichotomy

We can now summarize the previous study in a single statement.

Theorem 6.26

(Hopf-Tsuji-Sullivan theorem)
.

Let
X
be a proper, geodesic, metric space.
Fix a base point
o\in X
.
Let
G
be a group acting properly, by isometries on
X
.
We assume that
G
is not virtually cyclic and contains a contracting element.
Let
\omega\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
.
Let
\nu=(\nu_{x})
be a
G
-invariant,
\omega
-conformal density supported on
\partial X
.
Let
m_{G}
be the associated Bowen-Margulis measure on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G})
.
Then one of the following two cases holds.
Moreover, for each one, all the stated properties are equivalent.

—

Convergent case.

(c1)

The Poincaré series
\mathcal{P}_{G}(s)
converges at
s=\omega
.

(c2)

The measure
\nu_{o}
gives zero measure to the radial limit set.

(c3)

The diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial_{0}^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is dissipative.

(c4)

The geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is dissipative.

—

Divergent case.

(d1)

The Poincaré series
\mathcal{P}_{G}(s)
diverges at
s=\omega
, hence
\omega=\omega_{G}
.

(d2)

The measure
\nu_{o}
gives full measure to the contracting limit set, hence to the radial limit set.

(d3)

The diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial X\times\partial X,\mathfrak{B}\otimes\mathfrak{B},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is conservative.

(d4)

The geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative.

(d5)

The diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial X\times\partial X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is ergodic.

(d6)

The geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is ergodic.

Proof.

The equivalences
(d1)

\Leftrightarrow

(d2)
and
(c1)

\Leftrightarrow

(c2)
are proven in
[
9
]
.

Divergent case.

Let us state a couple of additional properties.

(d7)

The diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is conservative.

(d8)

The geodesic flow on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{G},m_{G})
is conservative.

(d9)

The diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is ergodic.

The proof now follows the plan below.
(d3)
(d7)
(d9)
(d6)
(d1)
(d2)
(d8)
(d5)
(d4)

The implications
(d3)

\Rightarrow

(d7)
,
(d4)

\Rightarrow

(d8)
and
(d5)

\Rightarrow

(d9)
are straightforward.

Proposition 6.11
shows
(d2)

\Rightarrow

(d3)
and
(d2)

\Rightarrow

(d4)
, while
Proposition 6.15
gives

(d7)

\Rightarrow

(d2)
and
(d8)

\Rightarrow

(d2)
.
The implication
(d8)

\Rightarrow

(d5)
follows from
Theorem 6.16
.

We now prove
(d9)

\Rightarrow

(d7)
.
Suppose that diagonal action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
.
It follows from
Proposition 6.25
that
(\partial X,\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o})
has no atom.
Hence neither has
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
.
However since
G
is countable, it implies that the action of
G
on
(\partial^{2}X,\mathfrak{B}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{B}^{+},\nu_{o}\otimes\nu_{o})
is conservative.

We can endow
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+},m)
with an action of
G\times\mathbf{R}
, where the action
\mathbf{R}
corresponds to the geodesic flow.
Unwrapping the definitions, one observes that
(d6)
and
(d9)
are equivalent to the ergodicity of the action of
G\times\mathbf{R}
on
(SX,\mathfrak{L}^{+},m)
is ergodic.
This proves
(d6)

\Leftrightarrow

(d9)
.

Convergent case.

Proposition 6.14
gives
(c2)

\Rightarrow

(c3)
and
(c2)

\Rightarrow

(c4)
.
The converse implications were already proved during the divergent case.
∎
